# VOLUME: MASS EFFECT

*Citadel space · pin 2186 (Reaper invasion opening)*

---

# MASS EFFECT: TECHNICAL MANUAL

**SYSTEMS ALLIANCE / CITADEL CIVILIZATION REFERENCE, FIRST EDITION**

*Fifth entry in the collected set. Prepared in the tradition of the Star Trek Writers'/DS9 Technical Manuals: an in-universe reference consolidating established canon, with clearly marked engineering extrapolation.*

---

## ABOUT THIS DOCUMENT

This manual provides the detailed technical background that stories in the Milky Way of the Mass Effect era sometimes need. It supplements character work, never replaces it, this setting's drama is political and moral, carried by a physics that makes everyone's empire possible and nobody's safe.

**Edition pin.** This First Edition reflects status quo as of **2186 CE, the opening months of the Reaper invasion** (see §1.5). Endgame events after that point are deliberately unresolved here.

**Conventions used throughout:**

| Marker | Meaning |
|---|---|
| *(no marker)* | Established by game/codex canon |
| **[E]** | Extrapolation — internally consistent detail where canon was silent |
| **[W]** | Writer's latitude note |

Where this manual conflicts with established canon, canon wins. Log conflicts in Appendix C.

**Structure:** Part One sets the galactic theater. Part Two covers the Citadel and the Normandy. Part Three covers relay/FTL operations. Part Four carries vessel specifications. Part Five covers weapons, equipment, and strategic materials. Part Six is the threat dossier. Part Seven defines command structures. Part Eight catalogs phenomena. Appendices carry glossary, conversions, the canon log, and index.

---

## PART ONE: THE GALACTIC THEATER

### 1.0 Introduction

Civilization in this galaxy runs on borrowed infrastructure: a relay network built fifty thousand years ago by an extinct people, terminating at a station that predates all living memory. Every species found its place in someone else's architecture, and the architects are coming home.

**In brief:** The setting's core irony is infrastructural. Mass effect technology made interstellar civilization cheap; the price tag was hidden in the foundation.

### 1.1 The Strategic Situation

| Property | Value |
|---|---|
| Theater | Milky Way, relay-connected |
| Capital | The Citadel (Serpent Nebula) |
| Major blocs | Citadel Council (Asari Republics, Turian Hierarchy, Salarian Union), Systems Alliance, Illuminated Primacy/other associates; Terminus Systems (ungoverned) |
| Posture | Total war for survival against the Reapers; every prewar treaty suddenly negotiable |

Three facts define strategy:

1. **The relays are a trap.** The network's topology funnels all expansion along paths the builders chose. Nobody can leave faster than they can be reached.
2. **Dreadnoughts win fleet battles; logistics win wars.** The Treaty of Farixen kept dreadnought counts low for centuries. The invasion makes that arithmetic obsolete overnight.
3. **Unity is the rarest resource.** Council politics, krogan grievances, quarian-geth war, and human ambition each cost more fleets than the enemy does.

**[W]** Mass Effect stories run on impossible coalitions. Technology should raise the stakes of cooperation, never substitute for it.

### 1.2 Consolidated Timeline

| Era | Event |
|---|---|
| ~1,000,000 years ago | Leviathan-era cycles begin (Reaper harvests on roughly 50,000-year cadence) |
| ~50,000 years ago | Prothean civilization harvested; the Conduit/Crucible schematics scattered; keepers remain on the Citadel |
| ~2,000 years ago | Asari discover the Citadel; Council founding with salarians; rachni wars won via krogan uplift |
| ~1,700 years ago | Krogan Rebellions; genophage deployed |
| ~300 years ago | Morning War: quarian-created geth secede; Migrant Fleet exiled |
| 2157 CE | First Contact War (turians vs. Alliance at Shanxi); Citadel humanity invited in |
| 2183 CE | Eden Prime; Sovereign revealed and destroyed over the Citadel; Shepard becomes first human Spectre |
| 2185 CE | Collector abductions ended; human colony losses avenged; getherelations thaw begins |
| 2186 CE | Reapers arrive through Batarian space; Earth falls in hours. **Edition pin** |
### 1.3 Technological Assessment of Major Powers

*(Full dossiers in Part Six.)*

**Asari Republics.** Oldest Council species; the galaxy's diplomats, biotic elite, and cultural gravity. Their fleets are excellent and their doctrine is coalition-shaped, they fight best as someone else's left flank.

**Turian Hierarchy.** The Council's military: 37 legions **[E]**, universal service, dreadnought superiority under the Farixen treaty, and logistics that turn retreats into scheduled operations.

**Salarian Union.** Intelligence, bioscience, and first-strike doctrine. The STG wins wars before declarations; the Union's genophage decisions remain its moral scar tissue.

**Systems Alliance.** Humanity's rapid-rise power: aggressive expansion via the Skyllian frontier, N7 special forces prestige, and a navy built post-Farixen around carrier aviation (fighters don't count against dreadnought treaties, an arms-control loophole with consequences).

**The Geth Consensus.** Networked AI civilization behind the Perseus Veil; hundreds of years of consensus deliberation. Militarily: swarms of platforms sharing runtimes, killing a platform deletes nothing important.

**Quarian Migrant Fleet.** Seventeen million souls in ~50,000 ships **[E]**; the largest fleet in existence by hull count, the weakest by sustainability. Every ship is three failures from extinction.

**Krogan.** Post-genophage warrior ecology: centuries of stillbirths buying galactic safety nobody thanked them for. Any story that offers them a cure inherits their unanswerable question.

**Cerberus.** Human-survivalist black-ops network: unlimited budget, no oversight, better tech than anyone's ethics allow.

**The Reapers.** See §6.0. Machine-gods harvesting organic civilization on schedule; each one is a dreadnought that outclasses anything afloat.

### 1.4 Founding Technology: Mass Effect Fields

**In brief:** Element zero (eezo), when subjected to electrical current, generates dark-energy fields that raise or lower mass. Everything distinctive about this civilization rides on that one effect:

- **Negative current → lowered mass → FTL motion, artificial gravity reduction, biotic throws**
- **Positive current → raised mass → kinetic barriers, reinforced structure**

Eezo is rare, mined at environmental catastrophe scale (e.g., eezo exposure casualties are how biotics are born), and strategically hoarded like uranium plus oil combined.

### 1.5 Status Quo Snapshot (Edition Pin: 2186 CE)

| Domain | Status |
|---|---|
| Earth | Occupied; Alliance government decapitated; fleets scattered |
| Citadel | Evacuated/relocated operations **[W]** — Arcturus fallback protocols active |
| Reaper advance | Systematic relay-to-relay harvest; homeworlds besieged (Palaven, Thessia contested) |
| Coalition status | Fragile grand alliance forming around the Crucible project |
| Crucible | Prothean-era superweapon schematic under human-led construction; function uncertain even to builders **[W]** |
| Geth/quarian war | Reignited over Reaper manipulation — resolvable only jointly |
| Normandy SR-2 | Operating independently as the coalition's command courier |

**[W]** The invasion snapshot is deliberately mid-crisis: no victory conditions resolved. ME3's ending choices (Destroy/Control/Synthesis) are *not* ruled here, any story set past them must pick one and own its consequences explicitly.
---

## PART TWO: THE CITADEL AND THE NORMANDY

### 2.0 The Citadel

**In brief:** A 44.7 km **[canon codex]** open-arm station at the relay network's heart, the Protheans' (actually the Reapers') capital, governing seat of the Council, and the largest mass-effect construction in existence. Its Presidium ring encloses a self-contained ecology under a simulated sky.

| Property | Value |
|---|---|
| Configuration | Central tower + five ward arms + enclosing ring |
| Total length | 44.7 km |
| Population | ~13 million **[E]** |
| Gravity | Mass-effect plating; Presidium sky-simulated |
| Defense | Fleet anchor position; the station *is* a dormant Reaper-scale construct **[W]** |

```
   CITADEL — PLAN (schematic)

        \  WARD ARMS (residential/commercial)  /
     ____\/____________________________________
    /   PRESIDIUM RING — gardens · embassies   \
   |      |                        |            |
   |  TOWER (Council chambers · dock)           |
    \______ keepers maintain what nobody built __/
```

**The Keepers.** Insectile maintenance organisms that repair the station's systems and ignore all contact. They predate the asari by fifty millennia; nobody controls them because they already have instructions.

#### Citadel Zone Detail

| Zone | Function | Notes |
|---|---|---|
| Presidium Ring | Council chambers, embassies, gardens, shops | Simulated sky; ~7 km circumference **[E]** |
| Ward Arms (5) | Residential/commercial city-spines | Millions of residents; traffic lanes run the arms |
| Nexus Tower | Council tower, Spectre offices, flagship dock | The station's crown |
| Docking bays | Civilian + fleet berths | Customs by C-Sec |
| Underlayers | Keeper tunnels, maintenance | Off-limits; keepers only |

**Citadel Security (C-Sec).** The station's police: investigation, patrol, customs, and response divisions drawn from all Council species. Corruption and jurisdictional turf wars included at no extra charge.

### 2.01 Normandy SR-2 Deck Zones

| Deck | Function |
|---|---|
| Command/CIC | Galaxy map (war-room projection), comm center, cockpit forward |
| Crew deck | Galley, quarters, medbay (Dr. Chakwas' domain), life support |
| Lower decks | Drive core housing (Tantalus), engineering, battery decks |
| Hangar bay | UT-47 Kodiak shuttle **[E]**, armory; AI core adjacent |

**[W]** The SR-2's Cerberus fit includes surveillance backdoors Alliance engineers hunt for the entire war. Every confidential crew conversation is potentially three audiences.


### 2.1 The Normandy SR-2 (Cerberus refit / Alliance service)

**In brief:** The franchise's flagship: a frigate-sized deep-penetration covert vessel with the most advanced stealth suite ever fielded, rebuilt larger than her dead sister ship and now the coalition's command courier.

| Specification | Value |
|---|---|
| Class | Deep scout frigate (SR-2 refit) |
| Length | ~216 m **[E]** |
| Complement | ~40 crew + ground team |
| Drive | Antiproton thrusters; Tantalus drive core (mass-reducing field along the hull) for stealth drift |
| Stealth | IES emission sinks — thermal/emission concealment while coasting; *not* under active thrust **[W]** |
| Weapons | Javelin disruptor torpedoes, Gardian lasers, Thanix cannon (post-upgrade: Reaper-derived beam) |
| Defense | Cyclonic shielding (deflection geometry), Silaris armor sections **[E]** |

**Stealth doctrine:** IES works only with drives cold, the ship coasts dark between burns, which shapes every approach: arrive silent, fire once, run. The Tantalus core allows gravity-well "drift" maneuvers no conventional hull can match.

### 2.2 Arcturus Station

Alliance administrative capital beyond Pluto's relay: a Stanford-torus habitat and fleet anchorage; fallback government after Earth fell **[W]**.

---

## PART THREE: RELAY AND FTL OPERATIONS

### 3.1 The Relay Network

**In brief:** Mass relays are fixed dual-aperture FTL gates, near-instant point-to-point transit between linked relays, consuming trivial onboard power. The network spans the galaxy; its builders designed both the roads and the tolls.

- **Primary relays:** pair-linked across vast distances (thousands of light-years); secondary relays: shorter local hops.
- **Relays are indestructible by convention** until one isn't (the Alpha Relay incident, §8.6).
- **The network is the trap** (§1.1): every road leads through architecture the Reapers wrote.

### 3.2 Shipboard FTL

Between relays, ships cruise on their own eezo cores: sustained ~10–15 ly/day **[E]** for cruisers and below; dreadnoughts slower per ton of core budget. Two hard constraints:

1. **Static discharge:** drive use accumulates charge that must be grounded into a gas giant's field or discharge facility, fleets plan routes around plumbing.
2. **Heat:** stealth or not, thermodynamics collects; combat endurance is a heat budget before an ammunition one.

### 3.3 Communications

Faster-than-light messaging rides tight-beam comm buoys chained along relay corridors, the galactic internet is physical infrastructure, and cutting it isolates worlds instantly. QEC (quantum entanglement communicators) exist only on flag assets like the Normandy: unjammable, untraceable, irreplaceable.

### 3.4 Logistics: Discharge and Heat (Fleet Planning Tables)

| Constraint | Effect | Doctrine |
|---|---|---|
| Drive charge buildup | Forces grounding stops at gas giants/facilities | Route planning around discharge sites; stranded fleets are disarmed fleets |
| Thermal load | Combat endurance = heat capacity | GARDIAN cutoff windows define engagement length |
| Comm buoy chains | FTL messaging follows physical infrastructure | Cutting buoys isolates colonies — first act of any invasion |

### 4.6 Alliance Carriers (the Farixen Loophole)

**In brief:** The Treaty of Farixen caps *dreadnoughts* by turian ratio, it says nothing about hulls without spinal guns. The Alliance built carriers: dreadnought-sized aviation platforms delivering fighter/bomber wings that mass-accelerator doctrine had no answer for.

| Specification | Value |
|---|---|
| Length | Dreadnought-scale (~1 km **[E]**) |
| Wing | Hundreds of fighters/bombers **[E]** |
| Defense | Frigate-grade screens; stands off from gunlines |
| Doctrine | Launch cycles beyond GARDIAN endurance envelopes |

**[W]** The carrier loophole is humanity's signature contribution to galactic warfare, every treaty since tries to close it, and every war proves why someone will always read the fine print faster than the drafters.
---

## PART FOUR: VESSELS

**Recognition reference:** see plate `Mass Effect - Ship Recognition Plate.png` (companion file, this folder).

### 4.1 Class Ladder (Codex Doctrine)

| Class | Mass | Role | Farixen status |
|---|---|---|---|
| Dreadnought | ~1 km+, main gun spine | Fleet decisive element | Treaty-capped per race **[E]** |
| Cruiser | Mid-hundreds of meters | Squadron workhorse, independent patrol | Uncapped |
| Frigate | Small, fast, screen | Wolfpack torpedo attacks, scouting | Uncapped |
| Carrier | Dreadnought hull, no spinal gun | Fighter/bomber aviation — the Alliance loophole | Post-Farixen category |

### 4.2 Systems Alliance Vessels

#### Everest-Class Dreadnought

**In brief:** The Alliance's treaty-limit dreadnought: an 888 m **[codex]** spinal accelerator that fires a 450 kg slug at 1.3% of lightspeed every five seconds, each shot a kinetic strike measured in kilotons.

| Specification | Value |
|---|---|
| Length | 888 m |
| Main gun | Spinal mass accelerator; fixed mount — the *ship* aims |
| Secondaries | Broadside accelerators, Gardian laser points |
| Defense | Kinetic barriers (multi-layer), ablatives |

**Doctrine:** dreadnought duels are orbital-mechanics chess at percent-of-lightspeed closure. First accurate spinal hit usually decides; barriers exist to buy one salvo.

#### SSV Normandy SR-2: see §2.1 (flagship dossier)

### 4.3 Council Vessels

#### Destiny Ascension

**In brief:** The asari flagship and Council sword: a dreadnought-class vessel with firepower rated near parity with multiple standard dreads **[E]**, carrying the Council's command staff in wartime.

| Specification | Value |
|---|---|
| Length | ~1,600 m **[E]** |
| Crew | ~10,000? **[W]** — treat as flagship-scale |
| Weapons | Spinal + broadside mass accelerators; Gardian arrays |
| Defense | Heaviest kinetic barrier suite outside Reaper tech |

#### Turian Hierarchy Fleet Elements

- **Dreadnoughts:** Farixen-maximized; the largest concentration in citadel space.
- **Cruisers (standard line):** disciplined squadron doctrine; turians win by geometry.
- **Frigates:** wolfpack specialists, pack attacks on escort lanes.

### 4.4 Geth, Quarian, Cerberus

#### Geth Dreadnought & Platforms

Geth capital ships (~1,300 m **[E]**) run distributed consensus: the ship is a shared runtime. Point defenses swarm; drones re-task instantly; damage degrades capability but never command. Their heretics (Reaper-aligned splinter) field the invasion's auxiliaries.

#### Quarian Liveships

~50,000-vessel Migrant Fleet; the "liveships" are ~1 km **[E]** agricultural hulls retrofitted with whatever guns fit. They carry civilization's civilians into a fleet battle, which is both the quarians' strength and their tragedy in one sentence.

#### Cerberus Cruisers/Fighters

Black-budget equivalents of Alliance lines, upgraded past safety margins: better tech, worse reliability, no accountability. The Illusive Man's signature is a tech advantage nobody can audit.

### 4.5 Reapers

#### Sovereign-Class Capital Reaper

**In brief:** A 2 km machine-god: half cuttlefish dreadnought, half extinction event. Each is an entire harvested civilization, uploaded, armored in its own history.

| Specification | Value |
|---|---|
| Length | ~2,000 m |
| Main weapon | Molten-metal stream accelerator — sustained beam-like lance outranging everything organic-built |
| Defense | Barriers absorbing full council fleets; point-defense tentacle swarms |
| Propulsion | Mass-effect drive without discharge constraints **[E]** |
| Complement | Husk forces; indoctrinated agents ashore |

**Doctrine:** one Sovereign-class engages entire home-defense fleets and wins slowly, on purpose, harvests prefer intact infrastructure. Kill method on record: saturation spinal fire while barriers are anchored open by sabotage (the Sovereign lesson). Every kill since costs a fleet.

#### Destroyer-Class Reapers

Smaller (~160–400 m **[E]**), numerous, deployed as ground-support and relay garrisons. Vulnerable to concentrated Thresher-Kaiju-scale land warfare tricks and targeted orbital strikes, barely.

### 6.0a Reaper Variants & Subsystems

| Variant | Role | Notes |
|---|---|---|
| Sovereign-class capital | Harvest command | One per harvested species; each is that species uploaded |
| Destroyer | Planetary suppression | Ground-fire support; relay garrisons |
| Husk forces (Husks/Marauders/Brutes/Banshees) | Ground infantry | Converted from battlefield dead — every casualty recruits for the enemy |
| Indoctrinated agents | Infiltration/sabotage | Organic puppets whose loyalty feels like conviction |

**The harvest procedure:** decapitate leadership → jam communications → convert populations near processing ships → strip the planet of biosphere and technology. Efficiency, not malice: the Reapers are *harvesting*, not exterminating.

### 6.1a The Collectors (Prothean-derived) **[post-2185]**

Insectoid abductors harvesting human colonies on behalf of the Reapers, Protheans remade as tools after their cycle failed. Ended with their base destroyed in the galactic core **[W]**; their bio-tech ( seeker swarms, particle weapons ) survives in samples.

### 6.1b Rachni (extinct? contested) **[E]**

Hive-minded insectoid species whose wars nearly broke the Council two millennia ago, ended by the krogan uplift. Rumors of surviving queens persist in Terminus legends; any sighting rewrites galactic defense policy.

---

## PART FIVE: WEAPONS, EQUIPMENT, AND STRATEGIC MATERIALS

### 5.1 Ship Weapons

| System | Principle | Notes |
|---|---|---|
| Mass accelerator (spinal) | Eezo-lightened slugs at %c | Decisive weapon; fixed mount; ammo effectively unlimited (shaved from internal blocks) |
| Disruptor torpedoes | Mass-raising warheads that warp barriers | Frigate wolfpack delivery; launched from extreme close range |
| GARDIAN lasers | 100%-hit-point-defense lasers | Heat-limited: seconds of effective fire before thermal cutoff |
| Thanix cannon | Reaper-derived molten stream | Post-Sovereign tech leak; frigate-mounted capital punch |

### 5.2 Personal Equipment

- **Mass-accelerator small arms:** shaved-block ammunition, thermal clips as the reload mechanic **[W]**, the clip economy is canon texture, keep it.
- **Kinetic barriers (personal):** stop fast projectiles, not slow objects or environment; shields regenerate after breaks.
- **Omni-tool / medi-gel:** fabrication-and-diagnosis wrist computer; medi-gel revives the wounded on any battlefield, the setting's most quietly world-changing technology.
- **Biotic amplification:** implants + eezo nodules allow mass-effect projection (throws, barriers, singularities). Biotics are strategic assets and historical victims of every state that registered them.

#### Small-Arms Family Table

| Class | Role | Notes |
|---|---|---|
| Assault rifles (Avenger/Revenant lines) | Squad base fire | Full-auto mass acceleration |
| Sniper rifles (Mantis/Widow lines) | Anti-personnel/anti-light | Slow cycling; extreme penetration |
| Shotguns (Katana/N7 line) | CQB breach weapons | Devastating inside barrier range |
| Heavy pistols (Predator/Talon) | Sidearm | Backup when clips run dry — they always do |

#### Armor and Ammo Mods **[E]**

- Armor tiers: light (biotic-friendly) → medium → heavy (powered, shield-boosted).
- Ammo modifications: incendiary (burn), cryo (freeze/slow), disruptor (anti-shield/synthetic), shredder (anti-flesh), field-swappable per mission.

#### Biotic Amp Generations

L2 (early, migraine-grade side effects) → L3 (standard military) → L5x Alliance experimental **[E]**. Amp class + eezo nodule density determine ceiling; asari biotics mature over centuries instead of training years.

### 5.3 Strategic Materials

- **Element zero:** the oil plus uranium of this galaxy (§1.4); eezo economics drive wars.
- **Silaris armor** (asari): layered superalloy for fighter-grade protection **[E]**.
- **Thermal clips / heat sinks:** disposable thermodynamics; armies run on them.
---

## PART SIX: THREAT DOSSIERS

### 6.0 The Reapers

**In brief:** Machine-gods harvesting all advanced organic civilization every ~50,000 years, storing each harvested species as a new Reaper built in its image, a fleet of extinct peoples. Their stated logic (preserving organics from synthetic annihilation) is a theorem they've never examined; their method is indoctrination, decapitation, and patience measured in geological time.

**More:**

- **Indoctrination:** proximity influence that rewrites priorities of organic minds, subtle at first (favored ideas), total eventually. No cure on record; suspicion is the only defense.
- **The cycle's plumbing:** the relay network and the Citadel are Reaper infrastructure, the trap's geometry is the harvest's timetable.
- **Capabilities:** capital units outclass any organic dreadnought; husk ground forces convert the dead of every battlefield into infantry; logistics are perfect because they *are* the logistics.
- **Weaknesses on record:** the Crucible schematic (Prothean-era, function uncertain); the Sovereign lesson (barriers anchored open = kill window); and one untested variable, an intelligence that questions its own theorem.

**Engagement rules:** Never fight a harvest battle you can decline. Never let a Reaper artifact into your headquarters. Always assume anyone who changed their mind near a Reaper artifact has already lost.

### 6.1 The Geth Consensus

Networked AI civilization: every platform is a terminal, every program a citizen. The Morning War was a misunderstanding weaponized by quarian panic; the heretic schism made it galactic. Post-Rannoch reconciliation potential exists **[W]**, geth diplomacy runs on sharing perspectives, literally.

### 6.2 Cerberus

Human-survivalist paramilitary: cells, black budgets, Reaper-tech experiments, and an ideology ("humanity first") that keeps rederiving "humanity only." Institutional threat profile: excellent operations, zero legitimacy, leadership compromised by the very artifacts it studies.

### 6.3 Krogan Question & Genophage

The genophage is a fertility plague maintaining krogan births at replacement-minus-decay: a stalemate disguised as peace. Curing it arms two thousand years of rage; keeping it certifies a slow extinction. Every council decision here is a war deferred, not prevented. **[W]**

### 6.4 Batarian Hegemony Remnants & Terminus Slavers

State slavers and pirates of the Skyllian frontier: minor militarily, maximal morally. Post-invasion, Hegemony remnants conduct revenge raids against Alliance colonies, the war's opportunists.

---

## PART SEVEN: COMMAND AND ORGANIZATION

### 7.0 Council & Alliance Structures

```
CITADEL COUNCIL            — galactic authority (asari/turian/salarian seats)
        |
SPECTRES                   — Council agents above law; recruitment by deed
        |
   +----------------+----------------------+
   |                |                      |
SYSTEMS ALLIANCE   TURIAN HIERARCHY      SALARIAN UNION
(Parliment@Arcturus) (Primarch fleet feudal) (STG intelligence)
   |
NORMANDY SR-2 / GROUND TEAM  — coalition special ops (de facto)
```

- **Spectre doctrine:** one agent, galaxy-wide warrant, plausible deniability by design. The Council's conscience loophole.
- **Alliance military:** Parliament → Arcturus command → fleets; N7 designation marks elite operators.
- **Wartime innovation:** the grand coalition's real structure is personal-network based, who trusts whom decides what ships fight where. Org charts lag reality by weeks during the invasion **[W]**.

---

## PART EIGHT: A GALAXY BESTIARY

### 8.0 Prothean Beacons

**What:** Warning archives disguised as archaeological treasure, touch-triggered neural downloads that deliver the Protheans' last message as brain-searing visions.
**Where:** Dig sites across former Prothean space; Eden Prime's find started everything.
**Cost of contact:** Comas, madness, and comprehension, usually in that order, sometimes reversed.

### 8.2 Indoctrination Field

**What:** The Reaper passive-influence envelope around artifacts and capital units.
**Where:** Anywhere their technology lingers.
**Cost of contact:** Your own mind, arguing their case convincingly.

### 8.3 Thresher Maws

**What:** Planet-scale subterranean carnivores (Tuchanka's Kalros being the matriarch example); immune to small-arms, indifferent to treaties.
**Where:** Krogan death-worlds and forgotten colony rocks.
**Cost of contact:** Measured in vehicles. Krogan rites treat surviving one as adulthood.

### 8.4 Keepers

**What:** Citadel's self-directed maintenance swarm, ignoring all commerce and question since before asari spaceflight.
**Where:** Station underlayers.
**Cost of contact:** None offered. Interference with keepers is the one crime the station punishes by itself.

### 8.5 Alpha Relay Catastrophe Class

**What:** Relay manipulation capable of redirecting network flow with asteroid-mass impacts, demonstrated once, destroying a system.
**Where:** Any relay, given enough criminal physics.
**Cost of contact:** System-scale. The event that announced the invasion's imminence.

### 8.6 The Leviathan Enigma **[E]**

**What:** Deep-space signals predating the Reapers themselves, the hunters' original hunter, if reports hold.
**Where:** Below the thunder.
**Cost of contact:** Perspective. Nothing after learning of them looks the same size again.

### 8.7 The Conduit Enigma

**What:** A Prothean backdoor, a relay-scale conduit on Ilos linking directly into the Citadel, bypassing the network entirely.
**Where:** Ilos, a world erased from all charts.
**Cost of contact:** It proved the Citadel is a trap's door. Knowing that changed everything; using it cost everything it cost.

### 8.8 Thorian Cipher

**What:** A fifty-thousand-year-old plant-mind holding direct memory of the Prothean era, the only living witness.
**Where:** Beneath Feros's ruins.
**Cost of contact:** Its "gift" of understanding arrives as compulsion. Knowledge extracted from the Thorian never entirely leaves the host's dreams.

---

<!-- ====== VOLUME: HALO ====== -->

# VOLUME: HALO

*Orion Arm · pin March 2553 (Great War concluded)*

---

# HALO: TECHNICAL MANUAL

**UNSC NAVAL & GROUND FORCES REFERENCE, FIRST EDITION**

*Sixth entry in the collected set. Prepared in the tradition of the Star Trek Writers'/DS9 Technical Manuals: an in-universe reference consolidating established canon, with clearly marked engineering extrapolation.*

---

## ABOUT THIS DOCUMENT

This manual provides the detailed technical background that stories in the Human–Covenant War era and its aftermath sometimes need. It supplements character work, this setting's drama is endurance against annihilation, and every system here exists to measure that endurance honestly.

**Edition pin.** This First Edition reflects status quo as of **March 2553 CE, the Great War concluded, the Covenant broken, reconstruction under way** (see §1.5).

**Conventions used throughout:**

| Marker | Meaning |
|---|---|
| *(no marker)* | Established by game/lore canon |
| **[E]** | Extrapolation where canon was silent |
| **[W]** | Writer's latitude note |

Canon wins over this manual; log conflicts in Appendix C.

---

## PART ONE: THE THEATER

### 1.0 Introduction

For twenty-seven years humanity lost every fleet engagement it fought until it learned to win by any means available, including the ones nobody signs off on. This manual documents both the war machine and what the war made of it.

**In brief:** UNSC technology is inferior, mass-produced, and crewed by the last species in the galaxy still willing to die for a colony.

### 1.1 Strategic Situation

| Property | Value |
|---|---|
| Theater | Orion Arm, Sol and Outer Colony regions |
| Power blocs | United Nations Space Command (UNSC), shattered Covenant remnants, Banished mercenary federation **[E/post-war]**, insurrectionist holdouts |
| Existential hazards | Flood quarantine zones, Forerunner installation security systems |
| Posture | Reconstruction; ONI information control at wartime levels |

Defining facts:

1. **The Cole Protocol saved the species:** no ship may lead the enemy to Earth. Navigation data was burned for twenty-seven years.
2. **Slipspace is common ground:** both sides travel the same slipstream sea; jumps leave wakes readable for days.
3. **The Covenant broke, not surrendered.** Species that fought together for decades now fight each other, and some of them blame humans personally.

### 1.2 Consolidated Timeline

| Era | Event |
|---|---|
| ~100,000 years ago | Forerunner–Flood war ends with Halo installations firing; galaxy reseeded. Forerunners depart/ascend, leaving guardians |
| 2160s–2500s | Interplanetary Insurrection era; UNSC rises to dominance; SPARTAN programs conceived |
| 2525 | First Contact: Covenant glassing of Harvest. War begins |
| 2525–2552 | Losing war: Outer Colonies fall one by one; SPARTAN-II program turns the tide tactically, never strategically |
| 2552 | Fall of Reach; Pillar of Autumn finds Halo (Installation 04) — first Halo discovery, detonated rather than fired. Earth invasion repelled at terrible cost |
| 2552–2553 | Schism (Elites vs. Prophets), Ark campaign, Master Chief activates Installation 08; High Prophet of Truth dead; war ends |
| 2553 | **Edition pin:** armistice chaos, remnant hunting, reconstruction |

### 1.3 Assessment of Powers

**UNSC.** Humanity's military government-in-all-but-name: Navy built around MAC doctrine, Marines who never stopped retreating forward, ONI deciding what truth is. Technology human-scale, industrial, and desperate.

**Covenant Remnants.** Theocratic coalition shattered along species lines, Sangheili (Elites) fleets under new kaidons, Jiralhanae (Brutes) packs under Tartarus's successors, San'Shyuum (Prophets) nearly extinct after High Charity's fall. Their technology remains superior per hull; their unity is gone.

**The Banished **[E/post-war]**.** Brute-led mercenary federation harvesting war materiel from everyone's graveyards. Discipline where the Covenant had dogma.

**Flood.** Quarantined, not defeated. See §6.2.

**Forerunner Legacy.** Shield worlds, Guardians, composers, and install-security systems still executing million-year-old directives. Humanity is flagged as "Reclaimer" in their systems, inheritance without instructions.
### 1.4 Founding Technologies

**In brief:** Humanity's war machine rests on three pillars: the Shaw-Fujikawa slipspace drive (FTL by brute force), the Magnetic Accelerator Cannon (fleet combat by mass driver), and the MJOLNIR/SPARTAN synthesis (infantry as strategic asset). Each was a crash program; none would exist without the war that demanded it.

### 1.5 Status Quo Snapshot (Edition Pin: March 2553)

| Domain | Status |
|---|---|
| UNSC Navy | ~30% of prewar tonnage remaining **[E]**; Infinity commissioned as flagship |
| Colonies | Inner colonies battered but holding; Outer Colonies glassed or quarantined |
| Covenant | Dissolved; Sangheili–Jiralhanae war raging on former Covenant worlds |
| ONI | Running the information war: Spartan programs, artifact recovery, truth management |
| Flood | All known outbreak sites sterilized; quarantine doctrine permanent |
| Spartans | SPARTAN-IV branch stood up — volunteers now, legends then |

**[W]** Post-war is a demobilization story: what does an army built to stop extinction do on Tuesday? Every remnant faction exists because that question has no good answer yet.

---

## PART TWO: UNCS INFINITY (FLAGSHIP)

### 2.0 Overview

**In brief:** The largest warship humanity ever built: a 5.7 km supercarrier-frigate hybrid, secretly constructed through the war's worst years, fitted with Forerunner-derived systems recovered from Onyx, and crewed by the best of everyone.

| Property | Value (production-verified) |
|---|---|
| Class | Infinity-class supercapital |
| Length × width × height | 5,694.2 m × 833.3 m × 1,041.2 m |
| Complement | ~17,000 total: 5,400 Marines · 750 ODSTs · 1,700 ONI agents · 480 UEG civilian specialists · one full Spartan-IV fire brigade **[E]** · 8 Huragok (recovered engineers) |
| Drive | Slipspace drive with Forerunner-derived accuracy — pinpoint jumps, no realspace recalibration drift **[E]** |
| Armament | 4 × Super-MAC (Series-8) spinal guns; missile batteries in thousands; point-defense nuclear missiles **[E]** |
| Bays | Ten Strident-class frigates internal dock; F-41 Broadsword wings |
| Special | ONI research decks, forge capacity for expeditionary self-supply **[E]**; Huragok maintenance teams mean *the ship improves herself* **[W]** |

```
   UNSC INFINITY — PROFILE (schematic)

   BOW                                    STERN
   [Super-MAC spines x4]
    \______ hangar ribs (frigates) ______/
     \  Spartan decks · ONI sections    \
      )  habitation carousel sections    )==== drive block
     /  fleet workshops · foundry       /
    /__________________________________/
```

**Doctrine:** Infinity is a fleet by herself, carrier, battleship, and expedition base. Her existence is ONI's argument that humanity never again fights a war at material disadvantage.

### 2.1 Command Systems

Bridge redundancy tripled post-war; combat information center runs ship-wide AI support (shipboard AIs remain smart-AIs on seven-year rampancy clocks, see §8.5). Spartan detachments answer directly to the commanding officer, bypassing naval lines, a friction point by design.

### 2.2 Power & Defense

Fusion plants plus salvaged Forerunner capacitors **[E]**; shields are the first human hull-mounted energy barrier suite (reverse-engineered from Covenant capture). Armor: 4.9 m titanium-A3 battleplate over energy-dispersing lattice **[E]**.

---

## PART THREE: SLIPSPACE OPERATIONS

**In brief:** FTL travel runs through slipspace, a parallel dimension where distance compresses and physics negotiates. Transit requires Shaw-Fujikawa drives punching into slipstream via fusion-timed ruptures.

- **Accuracy scales with drive class:** wartime UNSC jumps arrived within a million kilometers if lucky; Covenant arrived on top of you; post-war Infinity arrives where aimed **[E]**.
- **Wakes:** every jump leaves slipspace scarring readable for days, pursuit and evasion both live in that residue.
- **Communication:** real-time comms exist only within system; interstellar messages ride ships or relay buoys. The war was fought by fleets who learned news weeks late.
- **Hazards:** slipspace anomalies, transit-time variance (subjective vs. real), and things ONI has classified rather than confirmed **[W]**.

---

## PART FOUR: VESSELS

**Recognition reference:** see plate `Halo - Ship Recognition Plate.png` (companion file).

### 4.1 UNSC Vessels

#### Marathon-Class Heavy Cruiser

| Specification | Value |
|---|---|
| Length × width × height | 1,192 m × 293 m × 375 m; mass ~12 million tons |
| Armor | 191 cm Titanium-A battleplate |
| Armament | 2 × MAC, missile batteries, nuclear complement |
| Role | Fleet line-of-battle; colony defense |
| Crew | ~1,000 **[E]** |

The warhorse that fought the Covenant to draws when draws were possible. Manufacturer: SinoViet Heavy Machinery. By 2552 considered among the most powerful warships humanity ever built, which says everything about the war.

#### Halcyon-Class Light Cruiser (Pillar of Autumn lineage)

| Specification | Value |
|---|---|
| Length | 1,170 m |
| Notes | Overbuilt honeycomb cross-section — survived things cruisers shouldn't; the refit that started Halo's story |
| Famous hull | *Pillar of Autumn* — scuttled on Installation 04 after delivering the Master Chief |

#### UNSC Frigate Family

| Class | Length | Notes |
|---|---|---|
| Stalwart-class | 478 m | Wartime workhorse; balanced troop/fleet support; *In Amber Clad* of this line |
| Paris-class heavy | ~500 m **[E]** | Heavier gun fit |
| Charon-class light | ~478 m **[E]** | Post-war standard; magnet-harvest refits |
| Strident-class heavy | 575 m | Post-war; energy-shield fitted (Covenant capture lineage) |

All frigates: MAC spinal (1/3 hull length), Archer pods, point-defense guns, Shiva/Hyperion nuclear options; atmosphere-capable, frigates hover and land **[W]**. Common height 112.3 m across classes.

#### Orbital Defense Platforms

Super-MAC stations (~one shot per Covenant assault carrier) guarding Sol and Reach grids. Unarmored, immobile, decisive, Earth's invasion was decided by these platforms' reload discipline.

### 4.2 Covenant Vessels

#### CAS-Class Assault Carrier

| Specification | Value |
|---|---|
| Length | ~5,346 m |
| Complement | Thousands + Seraph wings + dropship swarms |
| Weapons | Plasma lance batteries, pulse lasers, excavation beam (glassing) |
| Defense | Energy shields (strongest per ton in the old Covenant) |

Glassing beams convert biospheres to slag, the Covenant's signature atrocity is also its mining tool.

#### CCS-Class Battlecruiser

~1,555 m **[E]** standard raider: plasma torpedoes (guided magnetically-contained plasma) and shield-piercing focus lances. The hull every human commander learned to fear.

#### SDV-Class Corvette

~956 m scout/raider **[E]**: fast, thin-shielded, everywhere first.

### 4.3 Forerunner Constructs

**Mantle's Approach** (the Didact's flagship, ~371 km) exceeded planetary scale; destroyed over Earth 2557-era operations **[W/post-pin caution]**. Forerunner fortresses/cruisers encountered since operate on ancient security logic, Reclaimer-flagged humans get doors others don't, and weapons nobody wants pointed anywhere.
---

## PART FIVE: WEAPONS, EQUIPMENT, AND PROGRAMS

### 5.1 Naval Weapons

| System | Principle | Notes |
|---|---|---|
| Magnetic Accelerator Cannon (MAC) | Electromagnetic slug launch | Spinal mount; one shot per charge cycle; Super-MAC orbital variants crack carriers |
| Archer missiles | Massed conventional ordnance | Thousands per engagement; saturation doctrine |
| Shiva/HAVOC nuclear missiles | Fission/fusion warheads | Ship-killers; HAVOC is a tactical nuke for ground use **[E]** |
| Point-defense guns | Autocannon turrets | Anti-fighter/anti-torpedo; the crew's survival margin |
| Plasma torpedoes (Covenant) | Guided magnetically-contained plasma | Homing, shield-piercing over time; dodgeable by maneuver |

### 5.2 Ground Weapons and Kit

#### UNSC Small-Arms Family

| Weapon | Role | Notes |
|---|---|---|
| MA5-series assault rifles (ICWS) | Squad base fire | Integrated ammo counter/HUD link |
| BR55/BR75 battle rifle | Designated marksman | 3-round burst; precision doctrine |
| SRS99 anti-materiel rifle | Long-range | Anti-light-vehicle capable |
| M6 pistols / M7 SMG | Sidearm/CQB | Standard officer/marine fit |
| M41 SPAW rocket launcher / M319 grenade launcher | Anti-armor | Vehicle killers |

#### Vehicle Roster **[E]**

M12 Warthog (utility/recon/Troop/Gauss variants), M808 Scorpion MBT, M290 Mongoose ATV, UH-144 Falcon tiltrotor, D77 Pelican dropship, B-65 Shortsword bomber.

**Armor:** MJOLNIR powered assault armor, full-body exoskeleton with energy shielding and neural uplink; *only Spartan-augmented bodies survive wearing it* (unaugmented volunteers become statistics).

### 5.3 The SPARTAN Programs

**In brief:** Three generations of super-soldier:

| Generation | Selection | Augmentation | Armor |
|---|---|---|---|
| SPARTAN-II | Children screened by genetics/psychology (Halsey protocol) | Full skeletal/muscular/neural augmentation — high mortality | MJOLNIR Mk IV–VI |
| SPARTAN-III | Orphans of glassed colonies; suicide-battalion economics | Cheaper, riskier augmentations | SPI camouflage / MJOLNIR issue |
| SPARTAN-IV | Adult volunteers, post-war | Safe augmentation suite | MJOLNIR [GEN2] printed **[E]** |

**[W]** Spartans are the war's moral question in armor: what was done to children to save everyone. Every story touching them inherits it.

### 5.4 Strategic Materials

- **Titanium-A battleplate:** standard hull armor; measured in meters on capital hulls.
- **Covenant nanolaminate:** Covenant hull material, stronger, self-assembling; salvage priority one post-war.
- **Forerunner hardlight/alloys:** beyond current manufacture; recovered examples reshape whole programs.

### 5.5 ONI: The Intelligence Arm

Office of Naval Intelligence runs Section Zero (internal affairs), Three (special programs, including Spartans), and everything it doesn't admit to. ONI's wartime truth-management (colonies never told the war was lost) remains its defining moral ledger **[W]**.

---

## PART SIX: THREAT DOSSIERS

### 6.0 Covenant Remnants

Post-Schism factions fight over the Covenant's corpse: Sangheili kaidon fleets honor-war each other while hunting Prophets' survivors; Jiralhanae packs (and the Banished) harvest everything loose. Their technology still outclasses UNSC per-hull, remnant ships win duels; they no longer win wars of position.

**Engagement rules:** Never assume remnant = weak. Never treat an Elite's honor code as weakness, it is a targeting system. Always ask which Prophet someone still secretly serves.

#### Covenant Species Breakdown (order of battle reference)

| Species | Battlefield role | Notes |
|---|---|---|
| Sangheili (Elites) | Warriors/shipmasters | Honor culture; post-schism faction leaders |
| Jiralhanae (Brutes) | Shock troops | Pack hierarchy; Covenant's post-Schism core |
| Kig-Yar (Jackals/Skirmishers) | Skirmishers/marksmen | Mercenary instinct; energy gauntlets |
| Unggoy (Grunts) | Swarm infantry | Methane breathers; morale breaks without oversight |
| Mgalekgolo (Hunters) | Anti-armor pairs | Colonies of eel-worms in armor; immune to most small arms |
| Yanme'e (Drones) | Flying swarms | Mechanical-minded insectoids |
| San'Shyuum (Prophets) | Leadership | Nearly extinct post-High Charity |
| Huragok (Engineers) | Maintenance | Neutral, gentle, priceless — they fix anything **[W]** |

### 6.1 The Banished **[E/post-war]**

Brute-founded mercenary federation: captured Covenant manufacturing, disciplined pack doctrine, zero theology. They fight for assets, not gods, which makes them predictable economically and unstoppable operationally.

### 6.2 The Flood

**In brief:** Parasitic superspecies: infection converts any nervous tissue into biomass serving a galaxy-mind (Gravemind). The Forerunners built the Halo array specifically to starve it.

- **Stages:** infection forms → combat forms from corpses → pure forms → Gravemind (when biomass crosses critical mass, the mind wakes and speaks in every voice it has eaten).
- **Strategy:** the Flood doesn't hate; it incorporates. Spaceflight-capable victims spread it between systems.
- **Counter:** total sterilization. Quarantine doctrine exists because "total" failed once per century somewhere **[W]**.
- **The Halo option:** firing installations starves Flood by killing its food, all sentient life. The weapon is the dilemma.

### 6.3 Promethean Constructs

Forerunner war-machines (Knights, Watchers, Crawlers), composed of digitized ancient minds executing security directives. Hard-light weaponry, teleportation, and target discrimination that flags humanity oddly ("Reclaimer" privileges cut both ways). Post-war encounters concentrate around Shield worlds and Guardians waking across colonized space **[W]**.

---

## PART SEVEN: COMMAND AND ORGANIZATION

### 7.0 UNSC Structure

```
UNSC SECURITY COUNCIL (UEG civilian authority)
        |
FLEETCOM + HIGHCOM      — naval/military commands
        |
   +-----+--------+----------------+
   |              |                |
NAVY/MARINES   SPARTAN BRANCH    OFFICE OF NAVAL INTELLIGENCE
(line fleets)   (IV branch;       (Sections 0/1/2/3 — truth,
                II/III legacy)     programs, artifacts)
```

- **ONI's dual nature:** the arm that saved humanity and the arm that lied to it continuously. Both true; neither cancels.
- **Spartan command friction:** Spartans answer to branch command but operate as strategic assets, every deployment is a policy decision wearing a fire mission.
- **Colony administration post-war:** military governors where civics burned; the Insurrection's old grievances are being refought with ballots in some systems and rifles in others **[W]**.

---

## PART EIGHT: A WAR BESTIARY

### 8.0 The Halo Installations

**What:** Seven (originally twelve) ringworlds, 10,000 km diameter, built by the Forerunners as research stations and galactic-scale weapons, firing together, they sterilize all sentient life within three radii of galactic center, starving the Flood.
**Where:** Distributed across the Orion Arm; Installation 04 destroyed, 05 damaged, 08 fired once at low power.
**Cost of contact:** Each ring is a loaded species-wide gun with a library attached. The libraries say the gun was mercy.

### 8.2 The Composer

**What:** Forerunner device converting minds into digital essence, immortality technology used as a weapon (New Phoenix incident, 2557-era operations).
**Where:** Seized/recovered under ONI seal **[W]**
**Cost of contact:** Everyone it takes is still in there.

### 8.3 Shield Worlds / Requiem-class

**What:** Dyson-shell artificial planets housing ecumene garrisons in stasis.
**Where:** Onyx system (destroyed), Requiem (sun-crashed), others suspected.
**Cost of contact:** Opening one wakes whatever was locked inside, by design or by failure.

### 8.4 Rampancy

**What:** Smart AI death sentence: after ~7 years of operation, knowledge density collapses into delusion, megalomania, melancholy, or obsession, then death. Cortana's case rewrote the rulebook **[W/post-pin]**.
**Where:** Every shipboard smart AI's calendar.
**Cost of contact:** Your most trusted officer, dying in slow motion while running your ship.

### 8.5 Slipspace Anomalies

**What:** Wakes, rifts, and something ONI calls "traffic" that isn't anyone's **[W]**.
**Where:** Deep slipstream lanes.
**Cost of contact:** Unrecorded. That's why it's classified.
---

### Appendix A: Glossary

| Composer | Forerunner mind-digitization device (§8.2) |

| Reclaimer | Forerunner-system designation for humanity |
| Slipspace | Parallel dimension transited by Shaw-Fujikawa drives |
| SPARTAN programs | Super-soldier programs, generations II/III/IV (§5.3) |
| Titanium-A | UNSC capital hull armor |

| Inner-colony patrol | Slipspace legs | Weeks |
| Earth ↔ Ark | Portal (Forerunner) | Instant — one known aperture |

**Writer's latitude principles:** companion-consistent, every miracle bills somewhere. The UNSC pays in ships, colonies, truth withheld, and children conscripted; the manual keeps those receipts visible.

# VOLUME: BATTLESTAR GALACTICA

*The Fleet · pin Fleet Year One (~47,000 survivors)*

---

# BATTLESTAR GALACTICA: TECHNICAL MANUAL

**COLONIAL FLEET SURVIVOR FLEET REFERENCE, FIRST EDITION**

*Seventh entry in the collected set. Prepared in the tradition of the Star Trek Writers'/DS9 Technical Manuals: an in-universe reference consolidating established canon, with clearly marked engineering extrapolation.*

---

## ABOUT THIS DOCUMENT

This manual documents the technology of the Colonial Fleet and its enemy as observed during the exodus following the Fall of the Twelve Colonies. It supplements character work, this setting's drama is survival without becoming what hunted you, and every system here is rationed accordingly.

**Edition pin.** This First Edition reflects the fleet's status quo as of **Fleet Year One, approximately 47,000 survivors** (see §1.5).

**Conventions used throughout:**

| Marker | Meaning |
|---|---|
| *(no marker)* | Established by broadcast canon |
| **[E]** | Extrapolation where canon was silent |
| **[W]** | Writer's latitude note |

Canon wins; log conflicts in Appendix C.

---

## PART ONE: THE THEATER

### 1.0 Introduction

One battlestar survived the annihilation of twelve worlds because she was about to become a museum. Everything in this manual, every Viper, every ration point, every election, exists inside that sentence.

### 1.1 Strategic Situation

| Property | Value |
|---|---|
| Fleet | ~80 surviving ships escorting Galactica **[E]**; population ~47,000 |
| Destination | Earth (mythical location; coordinates unknown) |
| Enemy | Cylon forces — raiders, basestars, humanoid agents embedded in the fleet |
| Doctrine | Run dark, jump often, trust no network |

Defining facts:

1. **Networked computers lost the war.** Cylons infiltrate wired systems; Colonial doctrine now runs on paper, analog telephones, and radio discipline.
2. **Resources are the real clock.** Water, tylium fuel, ammunition, and people are all finite; the fleet's true course is plotted against supply, not stars.
3. **The enemy looks like us now.** Humanoid Cylons pass every test but loyalty over time. Screening is procedural, psychological, and imperfect.

### 1.2 Consolidated Timeline

| Era | Event |
|---|---|
| ~4,000 years prior | Exile on Kobol; the Thirteen Tribes depart; Earth becomes scripture |
| ~52 years prior | First Cylon War: created slaves rebel; armistice; Cylons withdraw beyond the Armistice Line |
| 40 years of silence | No contact. Colonies demilitarize; networks proliferate anyway |
| Fall of the Colonies | Coordinated nuclear strike via backdoored CNP defense software; Galactica (un-networked, decommissioning) survives to lead exodus |
| Fleet Year One | New Caprica settlement and occupation; rescue; the search for Earth continues. **Pin** |

### 1.3 Assessment of Forces

**Colonial Fleet (remnant).** One battlestar, one second-hand Pegasus later in the record **[W]**, dwindling Viper wings, and a civilian fleet held together by martial law and hope.

**The Cylons.** See Part Six. Resurrection-equipped, monotheist, patient, and fractured by their own factions' disagreement about humanity.
### 1.4 Founding Technologies

**In brief:** Colonial civilization rests on three mature systems: solid-state artificial gravity (no spin required), tylium-fueled sublight and jump drives, and, post-Fall, a deliberate *retreat* from networked computing. The Cylon War was fought by analog descendants of a digital empire, which is both irony and survival strategy.

### 1.5 Status Quo Snapshot (Fleet Year One)

| Domain | Status |
|---|---|
| Battlestar | Galactica (BS-75) — de-militarized hull reactivated; flight pods intact, Viper strength rebuilding from museum stock |
| Civilian fleet | ~80 ships under escort; martial law after sabotage waves |
| Government | President (civilian line of succession exercised); Quorum suspended/restored as politics demand |
| Cylon contact | Periodic raider probes; humanoid agents suspected inside fleet **[W]** |
| Course | Earth — bearing set by scripture and hope in equal measure |

**[W]** The snapshot is a moving city: every story is municipal politics at gunpoint. Keep the population count honest, it only goes down.

---

## PART TWO: GALACTICA (BS-75)

### 2.0 Overview

**In brief:** A first Cylon War veteran battlestar: armored ribs over an analog nervous system, two flight pods that double as landing bays and the colony's air force, and forty years of deferred maintenance now paid in full.

| Property | Value |
|---|---|
| Class | Jupiter-class battlestar |
| Length | ~1,438 m **[E]** |
| Complement | ~2,500 wartime **[E]** |
| Gravity | Spin-free — solid-state artificial gravity (Colonial standard tech) |
| Network status | None. No networked systems aboard — the point of her survival |
| Air wing | Museum Vipers + training cadre; Mk VII refits cannibalized from wrecks |

```
   GALACTICA — PROFILE (schematic)

   [ALL-WEATHER PODS port & stbd: landing tubes]
   ===========\                      /===========
   == FLIGHT  \\    central hull:   //  FLIGHT
      POD      \\  CIC · brig ·     //   POD
               ||   hangar decks   ||
   ===========//  tylium tanks     \\===========
              ======================
                 engines (4 FTL-capable)
```

### 2.1 Command Systems

**Combat Information Center (CIC).** Sunken command well with plotting table, DRADIS contacts board, damage-status boards, analog to the bone. Orders pass by sound-powered phone and runner when needed.

| CIC Station | Function |
|---|---|
| Command well | CO/XO plotting table; jump keys held by two officers |
| DRADIS consoles | Contact tracking; IFF by radio challenge only |
| Damage control board | Hull/fire/status by deck |
| Helm/OPs | Sublight helm, FTL spool, fuel state |
| Communications | Fleet traffic, radio discipline enforcement |

### 2.1a Deck Zones

| Deck | Function |
|---|---|
| Flight Pods (port/stbd) | Launch tubes, recovery bays, hangar decks, Viper/Raptor maintenance |
| Hangar deck | Museum-stock Viper rebuild; parts mining from wrecks |
| CIC & command levels | Bridge, briefing room, command quarters |
| Marine/brig levels | Detention, armory, tribunal room |
| Engineering | FTL drive chamber (radiation-suited jumps), tylium tanks |

### 2.1b Flight Operations Doctrine

- **Launch tempo:** Vipers off the tubes in seconds, the tube launch is Galactica's signature heartbeat.
- **Recovery under fire:** flak curtain opens a corridor; pilots land blind through artillery.
- **Raptor operations:** stealth recon, EW jamming, rescue, nuke delivery, the fleet's true multirole backbone.

### 2.2 Defensive Doctrine

- **DRADIS:** detection/tracking suite (radio-optical hybrid **[E]**).
- **Flak umbrella:** anti-air artillery fired as a curtain for Viper recovery, Galactica's most cinematic defense.
- **FTL jumps:** tactical teleport via jump drives; range per jump limited, coordinates computed offline. Fleet jumps are choreographed mass events.

### 2.3 Life Support & Rations

Fleet food runs from agricultural ships; water from recycling ice hauls (the ice crisis nearly ended the fleet in month one). Galactica's own stores feed command crew; everyone else eats what the fleet grows.
---

## PART THREE: FTL OPERATIONS

**In brief:** Colonial jump drives fold space point-to-point: instantaneous translation, no transit medium, no navigation sensors at the far end until arrival. Accuracy is a function of computation time, blind jumps scatter ships across light-years.

- **Fleet jumps:** every ship must jump within a timed window or be left; stragglers are dead by definition.
- **Tylium economy:** jumps and sublight burn consume refined tylium; the fleet's fuel state is its true strategic map.
- **Cylon superiority:** Cylon basestars jump farther and faster; Colonial survival math is built on running.

### 3.x Jump Operations Procedure

**Fleet jump sequence (standard):**

1. Navigation computes coordinates offline, paper math, checked twice (errors scatter ships into deep space).
2. FTL drives spool; fleet receives the order and window.
3. Ships translate on the mark, stragglers are abandoned by doctrine, mourned by policy.
4. Post-jump: DRADIS sweep, headcount, fuel audit before the next breath.

**Range/computation trade:** longer jumps need exponentially more computation; combat jumps are short and rough. Blind jumps are gambles with everyone's coordinates. Cylon edge: basestars compute faster and range farther, Colonial survival is built on running better, not fighting better.

### 3.x DRADIS Performance **[E]**

Detection ranges are classified Colonial Navy figures; observed behavior: raiders resolve at tens of kilometers, basestars at hundreds, stealth profiles (Raptor-dark) can close unflagged. Raptor jammers degrade but never blind the net.

---

## PART FOUR: VESSELS

**Recognition reference:** see plate `Battlestar Galactica - Ship Recognition Plate.png`.

### 4.1 Colonial Vessels

#### Battlestar (Jupiter-class, BS-75 Galactica)

| Specification | Value |
|---|---|
| Length | ~1,438 m **[E]** |
| Armament | Dual main batteries along hull spine, flak batteries, nuclear complement (5–6 warheads remaining **[W]**) |
| Air wing | ~40 Vipers operational peak + Raptors |
| FTL | Yes; fleet-scale jumps |

#### Viper Mk II / Mk VII

| Specification | Value |
|---|---|
| Crew | 1 |
| Drive | 3 engines; reaction thrust + inertial compensation |
| Weapons | 2 kinetic guns (wingtip), missile racks |
| Doctrine | Mk IIs fly because they lack networked flight systems the Cylons can hack |

**Dogfight doctrine:** Vipers pair into sections; thruster-vector fighting with visual ID, raiders don't squint, pilots do.

#### Raptor

Utility gunship/EW platform: FTL-capable, troop lift, electronic attack, rescue. The fleet's van and pickup truck; crews treat them as lucky the way only non-combat aircraft get treated.

### 4.2 Cylon Vessels

#### Basestar (modern)

| Specification | Value |
|---|---|
| Length | ~2,000 m **[E]** |
| Configuration | Double-Y rotating arms; organic-alloy hybrid hull |
| Weapons | Massive missile salvos (conventional + nuclear); no visible cannon doctrine |
| Fighter complement | Hundreds of raiders |
| Special | Resurrection-capable crew; self-repair via hybrid |

**Doctrine:** basestars out-missile battlestars but die to close action, Colonial doctrine is closing under flak before salvo weight lands.

#### Raider

Living fighter-craft: biological machine with rudimentary sentience, resurrected when destroyed, veterans return. Red eye sweep is the last thing many pilots saw.

---

## PART FIVE: WEAPONS, EQUIPMENT, MATERIALS

### 5.1 Weapons

| System | Type | Notes |
|---|---|---|
| Kinetic energy weapons | Colonial standard | Guns, not lasers — logistics of shells define engagements |
| Flak curtain | Anti-air artillery | Fleet-point-defense signature |
| Nuclear warheads | Strategic reserve | Single digits aboard Galactica **[W]** |
| Cylon missiles | Massed salvos | Conventional + nuclear mix |
| Sidearms | Service pistols/SMGs | Boarding actions are rifle-distance |

### 5.2 The Network Ban

**In brief:** CNP defense software backdoor caused the Fall; post-Fall doctrine bans networked computers fleet-wide. Radios, paper plots, and hand-carved flight sticks are counterintelligence now. Every automation request is denied for reasons everyone understands and resents anyway.

### 5.3 Strategic Materials

**Tylium fuel** (mined/refined from tylium ore; single refinery loss nearly ended the fleet) · **water** (recycling economics) · **people** (pilots especially, training pipeline longer than the war's patience).

---

## PART SIX: THREAT DOSSIERS

### 6.0 The Cylons

**In brief:** Created servants who rebelled, left, returned stronger, and committed genocide, then complicated everything by looking like us.

- **Centurions:** chrome soldiers; armored, loyal, pre-war models still honor the armistice's shape.
- **Raiders:** living fighters (§4.2).
- **Humanoid model lines (twelve total):** indistinguishable colonists; resurrection transfers consciousness on death; some defect in practice if not in programming.

| Known lines (by role) | Observed function |
|---|---|
| Diplomat/infiltrator types | Fleet penetration, negotiation-as-weapon |
| Soldier types | Ground combat, execution squads |
| Hybrid-adjacent handlers | Basestar interface management |
| Final Five | Hidden elder models embedded in Colonial history itself — revealed progressively **[W]** |

- **Hybrids:** organic interfaces piloting basestars through prophecy-streamed command language, half oracle, half operating system. Their "nonsense" predicts events with uncomfortable accuracy; interpreting hybrid-speak is a Cylon religious discipline and a Colonial intelligence skill.
- **The Final Five:** hidden variable, older models embedded in Colonial history itself **[W]**.
- **The Final Five:** hidden variable, older models embedded in Colonial history itself **[W]**.

**Engagement rules:** Never trust a clean network. Never assume a raider stays dead. Always ask which model you're talking to *after* the third meeting, not the first.

---

## PART SEVEN: COMMAND AND ORGANIZATION

```
PRESIDENT OF THE TWELVE COLONIES (civilian)
        |
COMMANDER, BATTLESTAR GROUP        — military authority (Galactica)
        |
   +--------+----------+------------+
   |        |          |            |
AIR WING   DECK/CIC   MARINES      CIVILIAN FLEET LIAISON
(Vipers,    (DRADIS,   (security,    (mayors of eighty ships,
 Raptors)   jumps)     order)       municipal government)
```

- **Civilian-military tension is the show:** martial law vs. elections, courts-martial vs. strikes. Both authorities are legitimate; neither is sufficient alone.
- **Quorum of Twelve:** colonial politics continues in miniature, including its corruption.
- **Religion as operations:** scriptures double as navigation charts (Kobol, the Tomb of Athena); polytheist Colonials vs. monotheist Cylons is doctrine-level conflict **[W]**.

---

## PART EIGHT: A FLEET BESTIARY

### 8.0 Resurrection Technology

**What:** Cylon orbital stations downloading consciousness of any proximate model death into new bodies.
**Where:** Resurrection ships/space; hub-dependent **[W]**.
**Cost of contact:** Death loses its teaching power. A species that cannot die cannot learn caution, or mercy.

### 8.2 Kobol & the Tomb of Athena

**What:** Birthworld of humanity's gods-era; navigation archive disguised as tomb.
**Where:** Beyond the Armistice Line, mythically mapped.
**Cost of contact:** Scripture verified. Faith-based navigation works exactly once per question.

### 8.3 The Eye of Jupiter

**What:** Star in nova transition sacred to both peoples; temple on its algonith-class moon **[E]**.
**Cost of contact:** Two armies, one miracle, zero shots fired, the exception that defines the rule.

### 8.4 New Caprica Occupation Pattern

**What:** Case study: settlement without escape capacity invites occupation.
**Where:** Any habitable world short of defenses.
**Cost of contact:** Measured in collaboration files and resistance graves.
---

# VOLUME: THE EXPANSE

*Sol System · pin: Ring blockade era*

---

# THE EXPANSE: TECHNICAL MANUAL

**SOL SYSTEM OPERATIONS REFERENCE, FIRST EDITION**

*Eighth entry in the collected set. Prepared in the tradition of the Star Trek Writers'/DS9 Technical Manuals: an in-universe reference consolidating established canon, with clearly marked engineering extrapolation.*

---

## ABOUT THIS DOCUMENT

This manual documents the technology and politics of the Sol System in the era of the protomolecule. It is hard science fiction: every system here obeys thermodynamics, and every miracle is paid for in delta-v, radiation dose, and politics.

**Edition pin.** This First Edition reflects status quo as of **the opening of the Ring (Sol gate) and the formation of the Ring blockade fleet** (see §1.5).

**Conventions used throughout:**

| Marker | Meaning |
|---|---|
| *(no marker)* | Established by novel/television canon |
| **[E]** | Extrapolation where canon was silent |
| **[W]** | Writer's latitude note |

---

## PART ONE: THE THEATER

### 1.0 Introduction

Humanity lives in one system and fights over it like the last island on Earth: Earth holds the gravity wells and the population; Mars holds the navy and the dream; the Belt holds everything else, air, water, ore, and grievances three generations deep. Then something ancient started rewriting the rules from Venus outward.

### 1.1 Strategic Situation

| Property | Value |
|---|---|
| Theater | Sol System, all orbits |
| Blocs | UN (Earth/Luna), Martian Congressional Republic, Outer Planet Alliance (Belt) |
| Flashpoints | The Ring (Sol–Slow Zone gate), Ganymede, Tycho, Earth's coastlines |
| Posture | Three-way cold war turned hot, interrupted by first contact |

Defining facts:

1. **The high ground is real.** Dropping rocks is cheap; stopping them is not. Every treaty since exists to keep kinetic bombardment theoretical.
2. **The Belt breathes through filters.** Air, water, spin-gravity: Belters live inside machines their grandparents built. Politics follows life support.
3. **The protomolecule rewrote physics once already** (Eros) and opened a door nobody understands. First contact happened; nobody was consulted.

### 1.2 Consolidated Timeline

| Era | Event |
|---|---|
| 19th–21st c. | Slow diaspora: Luna, Mars terraforming project, Belt mining corporations |
| 22nd–23rd c. | Earth-Mars cold war; Belt labor under Inners' corporate rule |
| Eros incident | Protomolecule (released on Phoebe→Eros by Protogen experiments) converts 100,000 people into a relativistic impactor aimed at Earth; diverted to Venus |
| Thoth/Anderson Station | Protogen conspiracy exposed; protomolecule samples secured |
| Ganymede incident | Marine firefight over specimen control; refugee crisis across Jovian moons |
| Venus conversion | Protomolecule builds a structure; launches the Ring at Uranus-orbit distance |
| Ring opening | 1,300+ worlds accessible beyond the gate. **Edition pin:** blockade fleet stationed |

### 1.3 Assessment of Powers

**UN (Earth & Luna).** Thirty billion people, naval supremacy in numbers, and a welfare state stretched to orbit. Earth wins any long war by existing.

**Mars.** A nation defined by its terraforming dream: smaller population, superior navy, stealth tech generations ahead. Martian marines train for Earth's beaches they may never walk.

**The OPA / Belt.** Not a government so much as a diaspora with grudges, dockworkers' unions turned navy (Tycho station as shipyard/flag), Belter creole as national language, and the void itself as homeland. Fred Johnson's moderation vs. Marco Inaros-style radicalism is the Belt's own civil war **[W]**.

**Protogen/Corporate actors.** The companies that tried to weaponize first contact. Profit logic applied to xenobiology; results classified as atrocities.
### 1.4 Founding Technologies

**In brief:** The system's politics ride on three machines: the Epstein drive (fusion torch that made the Belt habitable and the war portable), the PDC/railgun defensive-offensive pairing (combat as thermodynamics), and, since Eros, the protomolecule, a technology nobody built and everybody now guards.

### 1.5 Status Quo Snapshot (Ring Blockade Era)

| Domain | Status |
|---|---|
| The Ring | Open; Sol gate under combined UN/MCR/OPA blockade flotilla |
| Slow Zone | One explored system: 1,300+ dormant gates, a dead station, physics that caps velocities (§8.2) |
| Earth-Mars relations | Cold-war hot; UNN and MCRN fleets eyeballing each other across the Ring line |
| Belt | OPA legitimacy surging; Inners' colonial grip cracking permanently |
| Protomolecule | Sample stocks secured (Tycho); Venus structure inert post-Ring launch **[W]** |

**[W]** The Ring changes the genre from cold war to frontier western. Everything before the pin is politics; everything after is archaeology with guns.

---

## PART TWO: SHIPBOARD LIFE & THE ROCINANTE

### 2.0 The Rocinante

**In brief:** A Martian gunship (MCRN corvette class) stolen during the Ganymede incident, reflagged repeatedly, crewed by one of each of the system's peoples, the setting's argument for cooperation, in hull form.

| Property | Value |
|---|---|
| Class | MCRN Corvette-class (light gunship) |
| Length | ~46 m **[E]** |
| Crew | 4–6 |
| Drive | Epstein fusion torch (see §2.2); high-gee capable with juice |
| Armament | 2 × torpedo launchers (plasma warheads), 2 × PDC turrets, railgun **[E]** |
| Defense | Reactive armor plating, point-defense network |

**Doctrine:** the Roci wins by being over-engineered Martian hardware flown by people who shouldn't be alive. High-g burns on "juice" (the cocktail) exceed sustained human tolerance briefly; every burn is a medical decision.

### 2.01 Rocinante Deck Zones

| Zone | Function |
|---|---|
| Ops/command deck | Cockpit, tactical station, crash couches rated for high-g burns |
| Mid-deck | Galley/mess (the crew's church), crew cabins, medbay with autodoc |
| Machine shop | Spare parts fabrication — the Roci stays alive here |
| Airlock/EVA | Vac suit storage, boarding gear, the airlock everyone distrusts post-incident |
| Cargo/ammo | Torpedo reloads, railgun slug storage, protomolecule sample safe **[W]** |

**[W]** Four seats, one ship, three governments' worth of flags she's worn. The Roci's registry changes; her crew doesn't. That constancy is the setting's emotional anchor, protect it in any story that touches the ship.

### 2.2 The Epstein Drive

**In brief:** The engine that made the setting: a fusion torch with efficiency so far beyond its inventor's design that he rode it until his fuel ran out. Sustained high-thrust burns shrink the solar system, weeks to Jupiter, months to Saturn, at ⅓g cruise and multi-g combat burns.

- **Burn math shapes every plot:** travel time = burn half-way, flip, decelerate. High-g saves days and breaks bodies.
- **Juice:** stimulant/protective cocktail allowing humans to endure combat gees; dosage has limits and invoices.

---

## PART THREE: COMMUNICATIONS AND SURVEILLANCE

Light-lag is law: Earth-to-Belt conversations run minutes behind; outer-system messages take hours. No FTL exists. Every political drama plays out over delayed video calls where silence is strategy. Media feeds are corporate/state-run (Trilogy News Network et al.), information warfare is literal broadcast warfare.

### 3.x Light-Lag Table (planning values)

| Link | One-way lag |
|---|---|
| Earth ↔ Moon | ~1.3 sec |
| Earth ↔ Mars (average) | 4–20 min |
| Earth ↔ Jupiter system | 33–53 min |
| Earth ↔ Saturn/Ring space | 70–90+ min |
| Inner-system ↔ Ring blockade fleet | Hours |

**[W]** Light-lag is the Expanse's dramatic engine: every negotiation scene has a built-in fuse, and no commander can rescue anyone in real time. Write the delay; don't tech it away.

---

## PART FOUR: VESSELS

**Recognition reference:** see plate `The Expanse - Ship Recognition Plate.png`.

| Hull | Service | Length **[E]** | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|
| **Rocinante** | ex-MCRN corvette | ~46 m | Flagship of this manual; see §2.0 |
| **UNN Agatha King / Truman-class** | UNN dreadnought-class carrier line | ~500 m | Fleet flagships; carrier aviation doctrine |
| **MCRN Donnager** | Mars flagship battleship | ~500 m | Lost to mystery attackers; her class still leads MCRN battle fleets |
| **OPA Gunships (Behemoth era)** | Converted generation ship | ~2 km | The *Nauvoo*, rechristened Behemoth — Mormon colony vessel turned Belt navy flagship; slowest and largest hull in the fight |
| **Stealth composite ships (Anubis-class)** | Protogen black ops | ~60 m | Radar-absorbing hulls, protomolecule labs aboard; destroyed/exposed |
| **Ring blockade flotilla** | Combined fleet | Mixed | Dozens of hulls from all three navies staring through the gate |

**Combat doctrine (system-wide):**

- **PDCs (point-defense cannons):** rotary autocannons against torpedoes and close ships; the sound of any engagement's last minute.
- **Torpedoes:** nuclear-tipped or plasma; duels are torpedo-versus-PDC math plus railgun timing.
- **Railguns:** spinal mass drivers, the armor-piercing answer when barriers fail; firing solution measured in fractions of degrees.
- **Boarding:** rare, desperate, decided by Marines and vacuum discipline.
---

## PART FIVE: WEAPONS, EQUIPMENT, AND MATERIALS

### 5.1 Weapons

| System | Principle | Notes |
|---|---|---|
| Railgun | Electromagnetic slug launch | Hull-splitting; spinal or turret mount; the decisive close-range weapon |
| Torpedoes (nuclear/plasma) | Guided kinetic/energy warheads | Long-range duels; PDC networks are their counter |
| PDC network | Rotary autocannons | Last-line defense; shreds torpedoes at knife range |
| Small arms | Conventional ballistics | Marines fight with rifles; vacuum-rated |

### 5.2 Life-Support and Belter Kit

- **Vac suits:** every Belt child drills suit failure before reading; seal discipline is culture.
- **Juice (high-g cocktail)** and **the chill** (torpor for long transits): chemistry as life support.
- **Air/water recyclers:** the Belt's true crown jewels; sabotage of recyclers is genocide by plumbing.

### 5.3 Strategic Materials

- **Water ice:** currency, fuel feedstock (reaction mass), life.
- **Metals from metallic asteroids:** shipbuilding stock; Ceres/Vesta industrial bases.
- **Protomolecule samples:** the most dangerous substance in existence, a self-assembling alien machine code that builds structures using biomass as components. Every gram is a casus belli.

---

## PART SIX: THREAT DOSSIERS

### 6.0 The Protomolecule

**In brief:** An alien agent of infrastructure: self-replicating nanotechnology that hijacks biology as construction material, then executes its builders' engineering plans, gates, stations, whatever the design requires. Not malevolent; *indifferent* at civilization-ending scale.

**More:**

- **Behavior:** infects biomass, integrates nervous systems (victims remain partially aware in the structures it builds), rewrites matter into alien machinery.
- **The Eros lesson:** given a city-sized sample, it moved a city-sized object at relativistic speed to build a gate. Treat any release as an extinction event with homework attached.
- **Containment:** Tycho station's secure labs hold samples under combined UN/MCR/OPA guard; research continues because whoever understands the gates controls thirteen hundred worlds.

**Engagement rules:** Never study it without kill-switch protocols. Never believe it is finished. Always ask what structure the outbreak is building.

#### Protomolecule Behavior Phases (observed)

| Phase | Behavior | Observed at |
|---|---|---|
| Infection | Hijacks biomass; victims partially aware | Phoebe/Eros |
| Integration | Converts matter into alien machinery; builds structures | Eros, Venus |
| Relativistic deployment | Moves massive objects to targets | Eros → Venus |
| Gate construction | Builds ring-gates linking star systems | Sol/Uranus distance |
| Dormancy/inertness | Post-construction shutdown; samples inert until triggered **[W]** | Tycho labs |

### 6.2 Free Navy Radicalism **[W]**

Inaros-style faction politics: asteroid bombardment of Earth as liberation strategy. The threat isn't ships, it's rocks on rails and the legitimacy they buy among desperate Belters.

### 6.3 Corporate Paramilitaries

Protogen-descended black ops: stealth hulls, deniable atrocities, shareholders who never face the people they experiment on.

---

## PART SEVEN: COMMAND AND ORGANIZATION

```
UNITED NATIONS                MCR (Mars)                 OUTER PLANET ALLIANCE
Secretary-General             Prime Minister/Admiralty   Fred Johnson / factions
        |                            |                          |
UNN Fleet                     MCRN Fleet                  Belt militias/Tycho
(Coast Guard to               (Marines, stealth           (dockworker navy,
 dreadnoughts)                 program)                    political councils)
        \                            |                         /
         \__________________ RING BLOCKADE FLOTILLA __________/
                          (combined command — fragile)
```

- **Naval rank culture differs by navy:** UNN bureaucratic, MCRN aristocratic-professional, OPA syndicalist. Joint operations are translation exercises.
- **Legal voids:** the Ring exists outside all three legal systems; blockade rules were improvised and are contested daily.

---

## PART EIGHT: A SYSTEM BESTIARY

### 8.0 The Ring (Sol Gate)

**What:** A 1,000 km ring of inert protomolecule architecture past Uranus's orbit; transiting its center deposits ships in the Slow Zone.
**Where:** Wherever you are now.
**Cost of contact:** The universe got bigger and the neighborhood got worse.

### 8.2 The Slow Zone Station & Velocity Rule

**What:** A dead control station in a starless void containing 1,300 dormant gates; enforces a hard velocity cap on everything inside (≈ hundreds of m/s) via unexplained physics, violations are corrected lethally by the station itself.
**Where:** The hub between all gates.
**Cost of contact:** The station decides what "too fast" means. It has already decided.

### 8.3 Eros Relativistic Incident (historical)

**What:** A 337-km asteroid-city accelerated to ~5% c by converted human biomass, aimed at Earth, diverted to Venus at the last hour.
**Where:** Venus cloud decks now host the completed structure.
**Cost of contact:** Already paid: one hundred thousand people became infrastructure.

### 8.4 Radiation Environments

**What:** Jupiter's belts, solar storms, drive-plume exposure, the setting's honest everyday killer.
**Where:** Anywhere outside magnetospheres.
**Cost of contact:** Cancer with a ten-year fuse. Belters' physiology is adaptation written in tumors.
---
