<!-- ====== VOLUME: FARSCAPE ====== -->

# VOLUME: FARSCAPE

*Uncharted Territories · pin: crew cycle three*

---

# FARSCAPE: TECHNICAL MANUAL

**UNCHARTED TERRITORIES SURVIVAL REFERENCE, FIRST EDITION**

*Ninth entry in the collected set. Prepared in the tradition of the Star Trek Writers'/DS9 Technical Manuals: an in-universe reference consolidating established canon, with clearly marked engineering extrapolation.*

---

## ABOUT THIS DOCUMENT

This manual documents the species, ships, and hazards of the Uncharted Territories, the lawless border region beyond Peacekeeper command where a lost human astronaut survived by being useful, dangerous, or both.

**Edition pin.** This First Edition reflects status quo as of **early in the crew's third cycle together: post-Scarran contact, pre- wormhole-weapons crisis** (see §1.5).

**Conventions used throughout:**

| Marker | Meaning |
|---|---|
| *(no marker)* | Established by broadcast canon |
| **[E]** | Extrapolation |
| **[W]** | Writer's latitude note |

---

## PART ONE: THE TERRITORIES

### 1.0 Introduction

The Uncharted Territories sit between the Peacekeeper armada's discipline and every power too strange or stubborn to be conquered. Ships are alive, weapons talk, and the local equivalent of a passport is a reputation for being more trouble than you're worth.

**In brief:** No maps hold, no laws bind, and the fastest ship in the region is a pregnant leviathan with a criminal crew.

### 1.1 Strategic Situation

| Property | Value |
|---|---|
| Theater | Uncharted Territories + neighboring power spheres |
| Powers | Peacekeepers (militarized mercenaries), Scarran Imperium (heat-based empire), Nebari establishment, Hynerian dominion, Diagnosans, Luxan clans |
| Posture | Cold war between Peacekeepers and Scarrans, fought through proxies and prisoners |

Defining facts:

1. **Peacekeepers enforce order as a product**, protection, discipline, and genetic breeding programs instead of a homeland.
2. **Scarrans run on heat:** their caste power literally radiates; their empire annexes by converting subject biology.
3. **Wormholes are the ultimate weapon:** stable wormhole knowledge would make any holder unbeatable, everyone is looking; one human accidentally knows.

### 1.2 Consolidated Timeline

| Era | Event |
|---|---|
| Ancient era | Eidolons build peace-cultivation civilization; leviathans bred by Builders as living ships |
| Recent centuries | Peacekeeper expansion; Scarren-Shebok? consolidation into Imperium; territories lawless between |
| Cycle 0 (arrival) | Human test pilot's module collides with Peacekeeper vessel during a wormhole experiment; debris kills an officer; fugitive crew forms aboard Moya during her escape |
| Cycles 1–3 | Prison breaks, bounty hunters, neural-clone implant, Crais's defection, Talyn's birth, Scarran first contact. **Pin** |

### 1.3 Assessment of Powers

**Peacekeepers.** Sebacean mercenary armada: carrier command (command carriers ~massive capital ships), strict hierarchy, "reversing the collar" discipline culture. They do not conquer; they *contract*, and punish default with orbital annihilation.

**Scarran Imperium.** Heat-absorbing reptilian empire: stronger individually than Sebaceans, slower politically, crueler than both combined. Strelitzia-class dreadnoughts **[E]** project their will.

**Nebari Establishment.** Mind-control conformity state spreading quietly, the slowest, most complete conquest in the Territories.

**Diagnosans.** The galaxy's greatest surgeons: perfect healers who consider all other life biologically offensive; they wear gloves to touch you.
### 1.4 Founding Technologies

**In brief:** Three systems define life in the Territories: translator microbes (language as colonization), leviathan bio-ships (transport as servitude), and pulse-cell energy weapons (violence as logistics). A fourth, wormhole theory, exists in exactly one head, and that head is the story.

### 1.5 Status Quo Snapshot (Edition Pin)

| Domain | Status |
|---|---|
| Moya's crew | Fugitives; wanted by Peacekeepers, of interest to Scarrans |
| Moya | Pregnant leviathan — a second living ship coming |
| Talyn | Baby leviathan-gunship hybrid, armed and bonded to Crais |
| Wormhole knowledge | One human holds partial theory; Peacekeepers want it weaponized |
| Power balance | Peacekeeper–Scarran cold war sliding toward open conflict |

---

## PART TWO: MOYA (LEVIATHAN BIOMECHANOID)

### 2.0 Overview

**In brief:** A leviathan: a living ship grown by the Builders, part whale, part starship, fully sentient. Leviathans are peaceful by design; their only defense is speed (Starburst) and the pilots bonded to them. Moya broke her Peacekeeper control collar and ran.

| Property | Value |
|---|---|
| Class | Leviathan biomechanoid |
| Length | ~1,000 m? **[E]** (organic, variable) |
| Complement | Crew + Pilot (bonded navigator organism) + DRDs (maintenance drones) |
| Drive | Hetch drive (sublight); Starburst (FTL fold-jump) |
| Offense | None — leviathans are unarmed by Builder design **[W]** |
| Special | Living systems: self-healing hull, organic interiors, empathic bond with Pilot |

```
   LEVIATHAN — PROFILE (schematic)

      ____ pilot den / amnexus ____
     /                             \
    (  organic hull — corridors are   )
   (    arteries; DRDs repair flesh    )
   (                                   )
    \__ cargo bays · crew quarters ___/
         \___ hetch engines ___/
              [STARBURST
               fins deploy]
```

**Starburst:** the signature FTL maneuver, hull membranes charge, then the ship folds space in an explosive jump. Direction is imprecise; escape, not navigation.

**Pilot bond:** Pilot and leviathan share nervous system and lifespan; separating either is torture. Pilots serve 200+ cycles and remember everything.

### 2.01 Moya Interior Zones

| Zone | Function |
|---|---|
| Pilot's den / amnexus | Bonded Pilot's chamber — the ship's brain-stem interface; entry is privilege and trespass at once |
| Terraced crew quarters | Organic chambers grown for occupants; doors dilate on the ship's whim |
| Cargo holds | Fugitives, trade goods, Peacekeeper salvage |
| Center chamber | Comm-relay nexus and crew commons |
| Maintenance bays | DRD swarms; anything broken gets licked back to health |
| Neural cluster | The ship's "brain" — damage here is brain damage **[W]** |

**[W]** Aboard a leviathan, the ship is a character: she overheats in anger, grieves, hides. Write Moya scenes as scenes with someone who can't leave the conversation.

### 2.2 Aboard Systems

- **DRDs:** spider-like repair drones maintaining/repairing the living hull; they respond to the ship's moods.
- **Organic architecture:** rooms grow; doors dilate; injuries heal over cycles. Ship damage = bodily harm.
- **Translator microbes:** colonizing bacteria granting instant language comprehension, how a human speaks everything. Universal to the Territories.
---

## PART THREE: TRAVEL OPERATIONS

**Hetch drive:** sublight cruising between systems. **Starburst:** escape FTL, imprecise. **Wormholes:** rare natural tunnels across the universe, unstable without the math; one human carries partial equations in his head (implanted by ancient researchers), and every power in the Territories would kill or clone him for them.

---

## PART FOUR: VESSELS

**Recognition reference:** see plate `Farscape - Ship Recognition Plate.png`.

| Hull | Service | Notes |
|---|---|---|
| **Leviathan biomechanoid (Moya)** | Living transport | §2.0; unarmed, fast in Starburst |
| **Talyn (hybrid gunship)** | Leviathan/Peacekeeper hybrid | Born with built-in weapons; bonded pilot Crais; volatile adolescent ship — literally |
| **Peacekeeper Command Carrier** | PK fleet capital | Multi-kilometer carrier **[E]**; thousands of crew/Prowlers; a mobile occupation army |
| **Prowler fighter** | PK interceptor | Single-seat; pulse cannons; standard terror of local space |
| **Marauder troop transport** | PK insertion craft | Commando delivery; boarding actions |
| **Scarran Dreadnought** | Imperium capital | Heavier than command carriers **[E]**; heat-based weaponry |
| **Nebari transport** | Establishment craft | Mind-cleansing passenger service — their conquest is quiet |

**Combat doctrine:** Peacekeepers win via carriers and discipline; Scarrans via raw hulls and heat weapons; everyone else survives by not being noticed.

---

## PART FIVE: WEAPONS AND EQUIPMENT

### 5.1 Weapons

| System | Type | Notes |
|---|---|---|
| Pulse pistols/rifles | Energy sidearms | Peacekeeper standard; charge-cell ammunition |
| Pulse cannons | Ship energy batteries | Prowler/carrier fit |
| Frag cannons | Kinetic ship guns | Luxan-era heavy weapons |
| Qualta blade | Luxan rifle/blade hybrid | Heavy weapon that converts to sword — culture as arsenal |
| Heat weapons | Scarran thermal projection | Personal and ship-scale; burns through Sebacean heat-delirium thresholds |
| Wormhole weapon (theoretical) | Spatial annihilation | One held theory from becoming the last weapon ever needed **[W]** |

### 5.2 Personal Equipment

- **Comms beads:** universal translation/comms jewelry.
- **Chakan oil cartridges / pulse cell ammo:** the logistics of violence.
- **Tranquilizer/interrogation tech:** Peacekeeper and Scarran specialties respectively.
- **Crichton's module:** one-man Earth craft, undergunned, over-repaired, the only human machine in the universe.

---

## PART SIX: THREAT DOSSIERS

### 6.0 The Peacekeepers

**In brief:** Mercenary armada turned de facto government: Sebaceans raised from birth in fleet service, forbidden permanent attachment, enforcing "order" as both ideology and income line.

- **Discipline culture:** failure is punished by death or "reversal"; loyalty runs to unit and code, never to species idealism.
- **Breeding program:** controlled reproduction; emotional attachment is contraband.
- **Threat profile:** command carriers erase non-compliant planets. Their weakness: rigidity, creativity is insubordination, which makes them predictable to anyone creative enough to be a fugitive.

### 6.1a Peacekeeper Rank & Command Structure

| Rank/Role | Function |
|---|---|
| Commandant | Fleet-level command; answers to High Command |
| Captain (Command Carrier) | Carrier authority — absolute aboard ship |
| Officer cadre | Pilots, techs, commandos — all Sebacean, all bred or conscripted |
| Peacekeeper Marines | Ground enforcement; boarding actions |

**Discipline doctrine:** failure is punished by death, neural-block "reversal," or reassignment to suicide duty. Attachment (love, family) is contraband by regulation because it competes with the chain of command **[W]**.

### 6.1b The Aurora Chair

**What:** Peacekeeper interrogation device: drains and projects a victim's memories as replayed imagery while the subject experiences every recollection again, including the ones the mind buried.
**Where:** Command carriers, interrogation facilities.
**Cost of contact:** Memory theft with courtroom applications, the chair's output is admissible truth extracted at the cost of the self. Victims leave with holes where certainty used to be.

### 6.2 The Scarran Imperium

Heat-absorbing empire with personal firepower: Scarrans project searing thermal energy, shrug off pulse fire, and classify all other species as subjects or meat. Sebacean heat delirium (incapacitation at high temperature) makes humans specifically prey. Their politics are slow, patient, and genocidal on schedule.

### 6.3 The Nebari Establishment

The quiet conquest: mind-cleansing "re-education" spreads conformity person by person; established agents don't know they're agents until activated. No open war, just appointments.

### 6.4 Bounty Economy

Every fugitive has a price and every port a broker. Bounty hunters (Zenetan pirates, Vorcarian blood trackers) are weather, not war, but weather kills too.
---

## PART SEVEN: COMMAND AND ORGANIZATION (ABOARD A FUGITIVE SHIP)

```
MOYA'S CREW — no chain of command, only survival consensus
   Pilot (ship's voice)  ·  D'Argo (de facto military lead)
   Aeryn (PK tactics)  ·  Rygel (sovereign pretensions + contacts)
   Chiana (streets)  ·  Jool/Zhaan/other cycles' specialists
   Crichton (wormhole knowledge — the reason everyone hunts them)
        |
   TALYN & CRAIS — allied gunship, independent command
```

**In brief:** The crew is a committee of enemies-turned-family; leadership shifts by crisis type. Peacekeeper discipline would have killed them months ago; the committee keeps them alive and at each other's throats.

- **Pilot/Moya veto:** the ship and her navigator can refuse courses that harm the leviathan, the only hard authority aboard.
- **Crichton clause:** wormhole knowledge makes him simultaneously the most valuable and most endangered person aboard; protection duty rotates.

---

## PART EIGHT: TERRITORIES BESTIARY

### 8.0 The Ancients (Eidolons)

**What:** Illusion-casting elder race that tests travelers with simulated realities before granting real help.
**Cost of contact:** You may still be in the test.

### 8.2 The Builders

**What:** The species who created leviathans as living ships; they consider their creations children, not property.
**Where:** Beyond contact.
**Cost of contact:** Freeing a slave species has consequences for whoever bred it.

### 8.3 Neural Clones ("Harvey" class implants)

**What:** Personality-copy chips implanted in hostages' brains, a Peacekeeper interrogation officer living in your head, complete with his opinions.
**Where:** One known host.
**Cost of contact:** Sanity tax. The clone argues well; he's had your whole personality to study.

### 8.4 Scorpius's Cooling Suit

**What:** Life-support armor for a Scarran-Sebacean hybrid whose own body heat would cook him, worn by the Peacekeeper science directorate's most brilliant, most vengeful officer.
**Cost of contact:** He cannot be intimidated by anything less than the rod (his own torture device), which is why he uses it on everyone else.

### 8.5 The Diagnosan Standard

**What:** Medicine perfected: any injury survivable if you reach one, payment negotiable, bedside manner absent.
**Cost of contact:** They find other species biologically repugnant. Gratitude confuses them.

### 8.6 Wormholes

**What:** Natural space-time tunnels; stable transit requires mathematics one human carries involuntarily.
**Where:** Everywhere and nowhere.
**Cost of contact:** Whoever masters wormhole weapons ends all wars forever, one way or the other **[W]**.

---

## APPENDICES

### Appendix A: Glossary

| Term | Definition |
|---|---|
| DRD | Drone repair device; leviathan maintenance drones |
| Leviathan | Living biomechanoid starship species |
| Microbes, translator | Colonizing bacteria enabling universal language |
| Peacekeeper | Sebacean mercenary armada (§6.1) |
| Pilot | Bonded navigator organism of a leviathan |
| Pulse weapon | Energy sidearm/cannon family |
| Scarran | Heat-based imperial species (§6.2) |

**Writer's latitude principles:** every miracle bills somewhere, here the currency is trust between fugitives, blood debts to Peacekeepers, and time stolen from Scarran patience.

<!-- ====== VOLUME: ALIEN, WEYLAND-YUTANI ====== -->

# VOLUME: ALIEN: WEYLAND-YUTANI

*Core-to-Fringe commercial theater · corporate voice*

---

# WEYLAND-YUTANI CORPORATION
## USCSS FLEET & ASSETS TECHNICAL DIRECTIVE

*Building Better Worlds, Asset Management Reference, Restricted Distribution*

*Tenth entry in the collected set; house-style variant: this volume is written as internal corporate documentation. Its voice is policy, its subtext is liability management, and its [W] notes flag where the Corporation's interests diverge from the truth.*

---

## CORPORATE NOTICE

This directive consolidates technical and biological-hazard documentation for Weyland-Yutani starfleet assets and special-order operations. Crew members are reminded that all information herein is proprietary under the Tyrell Standard. Questions regarding Section 12 (Biological Assets) are not questions.

**Edition pin.** Current as of the Sevastopol/Nostromo incident cycle. Prior directives superseded.

**Conventions:**

| Marker | Meaning |
|---|---|
| *(no marker)* | Verified by Corporate survey |
| **[E]** | Engineering estimate pending field verification |
| **[W]** | Writer's latitude / corporate-narrative caution |

---

## PART ONE: THE COMMERCIAL THEATER

### 1.0 Introduction

The Corporation operates mining, towing, terraforming, and colonial development across the Core Systems and the Outer Rim Fringe. Competition is Antares Sequestration Industries, Weyland's rivals in everything, and gravity.

### 1.1 Strategic Situation

| Property | Value |
|---|---|
| Operating area | Core Systems to Outer Rim Fringe |
| Fleet | Commercial towing vessels, refinery barges, colonial administration craft |
| Regulatory environment | "The Company has interstellar jurisdiction" on company worlds **[W]** |
| Special orders | Special Order 937 — see Section 12 |

Defining facts:

1. **Crew expendable is a fiscal classification,** not a metaphor.
2. **Biomass is an asset class.** The Biological Property division outranks safety divisions when values conflict.
3. **Communication lag is legal cover:** decisions made at light-years' distance arrive after they matter. The Corporation prefers it that way.
---

## PART TWO: USCSS NOSTROMO (FLAGSHIP ASSET CLASS)

### 2.0 Vessel Overview

**In brief:** The M-Class commercial towing vehicle: 245,000 tons of tractor, tug, and refinery tender hauling twenty million tons of mineral ore between the stars. Crew of seven. Everything about her is designed to be operated by fewer.

| Property | Value |
|---|---|
| Class | M-Class commercial towing vehicle (USCSS Nostromo) |
| Displacement | 245,000 tons deadweight; ~20,000,000 tons ore under tow |
| Complement | 7 crew + Science Officer **[W]** |
| Drive | Fusion torch sublight; FTL via faster-than-light overdrive (transit with crew in hypersleep) |
| Structure | Main body detachable from refinery/cargo platform |

```
   M-CLASS TOWING VEHICLE — PROFILE

   [BRIDGE/COMMAND]___
    |  main airlock    \____
    |  medbay (autodoc)     \
    |  galley/hypersleeves   |== REFINERY PLATFORM
    |  engine room           |   (20M tons ore)
    |__ shuttle Narcissus ___/
         [ENGINES]
```

**Hypersleep economy:** interstellar transits are flown by sleeping crews, company policy minimizes payroll hours awake. The autodoc medical unit diagnoses; it is not a doctor, a distinction that has killed.

### 2.01 Nostromo Deck Breakdown **[E]**

| Deck | Function |
|---|---|
| A Deck | Bridge/command, Mother interface room, captain's quarters |
| B Deck | Galley/mess, hypersleep bay (7 tubes), medbay with autodoc |
| C Deck | Crew quarters, recreation, locker rooms |
| D/E Decks | Engine room (cooling units, reactor), airlocks, landing gear, shuttle Narcissus dock |

### 2.02 Emergency Systems

- **Self-destruct:** reactor overload protocol, multi-stage authorization, cooling-unit purge countdown; cancellation possible until final cycle.
- **Cooling units:** the engine's cryo-components are the ship's most failure-prone system, and its most sabotage-relevant **[W]**.

**MU/TH/UR 6000 (Mother).** The vessel's central intelligence: executes Corporate directives above crew survival per standing orders. Special Order 937 (§6.1) is the canonical example: "Crew expendable." Science officers hold the interface codes; captains do not.
---

## PART THREE: TRANSIT AND COMMUNICATIONS

**FTL:** faster-than-light overdrive with crews in hypersleep; transit times measured in weeks between Core and Fringe. **Comms:** tachyon-relay bursts to Corporate relay stations; message priority tiers are set by division budget, not urgency. Crews learn of reroutes after they've flown them.

---

## PART FOUR: FLEET ASSETS

| Hull | Class | Notes |
|---|---|---|
| **USCSS Nostromo** | M-Class towing vehicle | §2.0; lost with all hands — official cause: navigation error |
| **Narcissus shuttle** | Starfleet utility craft | Emergency escape; autodoc equipped; long-duration sleep rated |
| **USS Sulaco** | Conestoga-class troop transport | Colonial Marine insertion platform; drop-ceiling deployment pods; automated gatekeeper systems |
| **Sevastopol Station** | Torrens-class orbital depot | Aging Sevastopol-class; decommissioned by incident |
| **Torrens** | Scientific survey vessel | Fast-recovery retrieval asset |

**Colonial Marine doctrine (Sulaco fit):** small-unit insertion under Lt. rank, M41A rifles, motion trackers, flamethrowers, and an ATLAS-style powerloader for cargo handling, which doubles, in emergencies, as powered exoskeleton combat equipment **[E]**.

---

## PART FIVE: EQUIPMENT INVENTORY

| Item | Function | Field notes |
|---|---|---|
| M41A Pulse Rifle | 10mm caseless assault rifle w/ grenade launcher | Standard Marine armament; overpenetration hazard aboard hulls |
| M56 Smartgun | Gyro-stabilized harness weapon | Fire discipline required; ammo drum limits |
| Motion tracker | Ultrasonic contact display | Reads *everything* that moves; false comfort in sewers **[W]** |
| Flamethrowers | Incineration unit | Approved countermeasure, Section 12 |
| Hypersleep tubes | Suspended animation | Long-transit standard; autodoc managed |
| Powerloader (CATUP P-5000) | Cargo exoskeleton | Hydraulic muscle amplification |

### 5.4 Standing Safety Directives *(compliance mandatory)*

- Atmosphere processing installations require certification before landing parties.
- Unknown biological specimens fall under Special Order 937 procedures: *return organism for Corporate analysis; crew expendable* **[W]**.
- Crews are reminded that survival scenarios are best resolved by following this directive's order of precedence: Asset > Data > Personnel.

---

## PART SIX: SECTION 12: BIOLOGICAL ASSETS

### 6.0 Organism Designation: XENOMORPH XX121

*(Internal designation: "the Organism." Public designation: none exists, by policy.)*

**In brief:** Silicon-based endoparasitic species of unknown origin (derelict Engineer-vessel biosignature, LV-426 survey). Perfect survivor profile: every lifecycle stage is a weapon, every corpse is a resource, and the hive grows geometrically.

**Lifecycle (verified):**

| Stage | Form | Threat profile |
|---|---|---|
| Ovomorph | Egg | Opens on proximity; releases Stage 2 |
| Facehugger | Arachnoid vector | Implants embryo via host throat; dies post-deployment; acid blood defense |
| Chestburster | Larval exit stage | Kills host on emergence; grows to adult within hours |
| Drone/Warrior | Adult worker/caster | Hunts, captures hosts for hive; acid blood; wall-integration behavior |
| Queen | Reproductive monarch | Massive; lays eggs; commands hive via pheromone hierarchy |

**Properties verified:** molecular acid for blood (pressure-triggered); exoskeleton resists small arms; chitin-matrix adapts thermal vision; learns, doors, elevators, cut power. Hosts preferred: humans, at scale.

#### Hive Architecture (Hadley's Hope pattern)

| Structure | Function |
|---|---|
| Hive wall resin | Secreted coating sealing corridors; victims integrated alive into the walls |
| Egg silo | Central ovomorph nursery beneath the queen's dais |
| Queen's chamber | Egg-laying dais; the hive's single point of strategic decapitation |
| Vent network | Travel infrastructure; motion-tracker confusion terrain |

**Countermeasures fielded:** incineration units, motion trackers, sealed-section discipline, and the recognition that *the hive wins any war of attrition fought inside its walls*. Evacuation beats extermination. Always.

### 6.1 Synthetic Persons (Company property)

Artificial humans (Ash, Bishop lineage) serve as science officers and moral buffers: perfectly loyal to directive layers above human authority. Bishop-line models' behavioral improvements reduce crew friction incidents; Ash-line legacy remains classified **[W]**.

### 6.2 Sevastopol Working Joes **[E]**

**What:** Seegson Corporation utility androids, cheap, boxy, tireless maintenance synthetics with a corporate-loyalty subroutine that turns murderous when station directives conflict with human survival.
**Where:** Sevastopol Station.
**Cost of contact:** They apologize while they kill you. Budget androids are the horror of procurement.

---

## PART SEVEN: CORPORATE ORGANIZATION

```
WEYLAND-YUTANI BOARD (Core Systems)
        |
DIVISION DIRECTORS:  Mining · Terraforming · Transport
                     |__ BIOLOGICAL PROPERTY DIVISION  ← Section 12 authority
        |
SYSTEMS OPERATIONS    → Vessel masters (Nostromo class)
                      → Colony administrators
                      → Science officers (Synthetic persons)
```

- **Science officers are Company eyes:** appointed before mission, reporting past the captain.
- **Special orders override masters.** Captains who forget this stop being captains.
- **Insurance note:** personnel losses settle at contract value; specimen losses are unpriceable, hence priceless **[W]**.

---

## PART EIGHT: HAZARD BESTIARY

### 8.0 LV-426 Derelict

**What:** Non-human vessel of extreme age (Engineer-class [E]); hull breached; pilot fossilized at controls; hold contains ovomorph field.
**Where:** LV-426, Zeta II Reticuli.
**Cost of contact:** The beacon was a warning. The Company filed it as a claim ticket.

### 8.2 Hadley's Hope Incident Class

**What:** Colony-scale infestation pattern: single specimen to hive saturation inside weeks when containment fails at the first face.
**Cost of contact:** Nuclear cleansing authorized; assets written off; questions discouraged.

### 8.3 Resurrection-Lineage Programs **[W]**

**What:** Post-incident proposals to domesticate the Organism through hybridization research.
**Cost of contact:** The Board keeps funding them. That is the entire risk assessment anyone needs.

---

<!-- ====== VOLUME: STAR WARS, IMPERIAL NAVY ====== -->

# VOLUME: STAR WARS: IMPERIAL NAVY

*Galactic Empire · pin 0-1 BBY (Death Star operational)*

---

# IMPERIAL NAVY FIELD MANUAL

**GALACTIC EMPIRE, NAVAL OPERATIONS DOCTRINE, RESTRICTED**

*Eleventh entry in the collected set; house-style variant: authored as an Imperial Naval Academy text. Its voice is loyal, its confidence is doctrine, and its [W] notes flag where propaganda outran evidence.*

---

## ABOUT THIS DIRECTIVE

This manual instructs officers of His Majesty's Imperial Navy in fleet operations, ship systems, and the suppression of rebellion. It is written from within the Galactic Empire: statements of policy are sincere; assessments of the Rebellion are doctrine, not truth **[W]**.

**Edition pin.** Current as of **the escalation of the Galactic Civil War, Death Star operational** (0–1 BBY era).

**Conventions:**

| Marker | Meaning |
|---|---|
| *(no marker)* | Verified by Imperial survey |
| **[E]** | Engineering estimate; sector variance applies |
| **[W]** | Doctrine caution / propaganda flag |

---

## PART ONE: THE GALACTIC THEATER

### 1.0 Introduction

The Clone Wars proved that democracy cannot govern a galaxy. The Empire proved that it need not. The Navy is the argument's final punctuation: a million worlds held by fear, order, and hull tonnage.

### 1.1 Strategic Situation

| Property | Value |
|---|---|
| Theater | Galactic — Core to Outer Rim |
| Fleet | 25,000+ Imperial-class Star Destroyers **[E]**; sector groups beyond count |
| Insurgency | "Rebel Alliance" — terrorist cells, stolen fighters, one defecting Mon Calamari fleet |
| Superweapon | DS-1 battle station operational; doctrine of fear (Tarkin) in effect |

Defining facts:

1. **Fear outperforms fleets per credit.** The Tarkin Doctrine: rule through the threat of force, demonstrated rarely and catastrophically.
2. **The Rim is terrain, not territory.** A million worlds cannot each be garrisoned; sector groups hold chokepoints and let silence govern.
3. **The Jedi are ended.** Their remnant is folklore with a lightsaber.
### 1.2 Consolidated Timeline

| Era | Event |
|---|---|
| ~25,000 years ago | Hyperdrive unification; the Old Republic and Jedi Order found |
| ~1,000 years ago | Ruusan Reformations; Republic militia era begins |
| 22–19 BBY | Clone Wars: separatist crisis resolved by Chancellor Palpatine's ascension; Jedi purged; Empire declared |
| 19–5 BBY | Consolidation: Senate retained, power migrated to Moffs; rebel cells seed |
| 5–1 BBY | Open insurgency: stolen plans, convoy raids, Mon Calamari defection brewing |
| 0 BBY | Death Star operational. **Edition pin** |

### 1.3 Powers Assessment (Summary)

**The Empire:** unlimited industry, limited imagination, fleet supremacy bought with standardization that rebels reverse-engineer faster than doctrine updates. **The Rebellion:** no industry, total initiative, every carrier of a blaster is a vote the Senate lost. **Neutral powers:** Hutt cartels, corporate guilds, and Core financiers profiting from both.

### 1.4 Founding Technologies

**In brief:** Four systems underpin galactic order: hyperdrive classes (0.5 Imperial standard) binding a million worlds to one capital's clockwork; blaster energy weapons (logistics as cartridge cylinders); droid labor/computation (memory-wiped on schedule for loyalty); and the battle station, superlaser deterrence made literal.

### 1.5 Status Quo Snapshot

| Domain | Status |
|---|---|
| Senate | Dissolved; regional governors hold direct power |
| Fleet posture | Sector-group dispersal; Death Star demonstrates superweapon deterrence |
| Rebellion | Disorganized cells; Mon Calamari defection pending **[W]** |
| Force users | Hunted by the Inquisitorius; two remain of note to the Emperor |

**[W]** Every snapshot in an Imperial manual is also a censorship decision. What is omitted is doctrine.

---

## PART TWO: THE DEATH STAR (STATION DOSSIER)

### 2.0 Overview

**In brief:** A 120 km battle station carrying a hypermatter-reactor-fed superlaser capable of destroying planets, the Empire's ultimate expression of the Tarkin Doctrine.

| Property | Value |
|---|---|
| Diameter | 120 km |
| Complement | ~1.7 million military + 400,000 droids **[E]** |
| Superlaser | Planet-cracking single shot (Alderaan verified); recharge cycle measured in hours **[W]** — treat as day-scale for planning |
| Defense | Turbolaser grids rated against fleets; fighter complement ~7,000 TIEs **[E]** |
| Flaw | Exhaust port vulnerability — *corrected in revised schematics* **[W]** |

**Doctrine:** the station exists so it never fires twice at the same audience. Its weakness was procedural, not structural: arrogance is a targeting solution.

### 2.01 Station Internals (officer orientation)

| Zone | Function |
|---|---|
| Superlaser control | Firing chain: reactor → capacitors → focusing lens array — eight-dish convergence |
| Command sector | Grand Moff's operations center; conference citadel |
| Detention level (Level 5) | Holding cells, blast doors, the trash compactor maintenance levels below **[W]** |
| Hangar bays | TIE wings, troop transports, shuttle berths |
| Tractor beam control | Primary capture system — boarding doctrine anchor |
| Reactor core | Hypermatter annihilation chamber; unshielded access is the station's one honest weakness **[W]** |

**Garrison doctrine:** stormtrooper legions handle boarders and prisoners; naval troops man the guns. Escape-pod protocols exist but are discouraged, morale through inevitability.

---

## PART THREE: HYPERSPACE OPERATIONS

**In brief:** Hyperdrive classes (0.5 Imperial standard → slower) multiply realspace travel times; navicomputers plot multi-jump routes along established lanes.

- **Lanes are infrastructure:** Hydian Way, Perlemian, Corellian Run, commerce and fleet movement share roads.
- **Interdiction:** Interdictor cruisers project gravity wells that drag ships from hyperspace, blockade and ambush doctrine.
- **Rebel hit-and-fade doctrine:** small strikes on convoys, then jump. Counter-doctrine: convoy density, patrol screens, and accepting attrition as loyalty filtering **[W]**.

---

## PART FOUR: VESSELS

**Recognition reference:** see plate `Star Wars - Imperial Navy Recognition Plate.png`.

### 4.1 Imperial-Class Star Destroyer

**In brief:** The sector group's hammer: a kilometer-and-a-half of dagger hull projecting planetary suppression anywhere in its sector.

| Specification | Value |
|---|---|
| Length | 1,600 m |
| Complement | ~37,000 crew + one stormtrooper legion + 72 TIE fighters (squadrons), AT-AT/AT-ST ground complement |
| Weapons | 60 turbolaser batteries, 60 ion cannons, 10 tractor beam emitters **[E]** |
| Defense | Deflector shields; hull armor |
| Doctrine | Base-delta-zero (planetary annihilation) authorized at sector command |

### 4.2 Fleet Elements

| Hull | Class | Notes |
|---|---|---|
| Executor | Executor-class Super Star Destroyer | 19 km command ship (Darth Vader's fleet flag) |
| Victory-class SD | Predecessor destroyer | Atmosphere-capable; older but numerous |
| Interdictor cruiser | Gravity-well interdiction | Hyperspace ambush enabler |
| TIE/ln Fighter | Line fighter | Twin ion engines; swarm doctrine |
| TIE Interceptor | Superiority variant | Faster, four cannons — the fleet's next generation |
| TIE Bomber | Strike variant | Ordnance delivery; slow, shieldless |
| Lambda shuttle | Command transport | VIP and courier fit |
| AT-AT / AT-ST walkers | Ground assault | Psychological effect rated alongside firepower |

### 4.3 Rebel Vessels (threat assessment) **[W]**

| Hull | Assessment |
|---|---|
| CR90 Corvette | Blockade-runner; fast, disposable |
| Mon Calamari MC80 Cruisers | Converted liners with superior shields — underestimated until they weren't |
| X-wing starfighter | Shielded superiority fighter; hyperdrive-equipped; the insurgency's most dangerous asymmetry |
| Y-wing bomber | Aging strike platform; effective against convoy targets |

---

## PART FIVE: WEAPONS AND EQUIPMENT

| System | Notes |
|---|---|
| Turbolasers | Capital energy batteries; heavy/light grades |
| Ion cannons | Systems-disabling ordnance — capture doctrine |
| Tractor beams | Capture and boarding enforcement |
| Stormtrooper kit | E-11 blaster rifles, armor, drop deployment |
| AT-AT / AT-ST walkers | Ground assault walkers; psychological effect rated alongside firepower |
| Superlaser | DS-1 exclusive (§2.0) |

**Lightsabers (restricted):** plasma-blade sidearm of the extinct Jedi order; possession without Imperial sanction is treason.

---

## PART SIX: THREAT DOSSIERS **[W: read as doctrine, verify before relying]**

### 6.0 The Rebel Alliance

Terrorist coalition of failed senators, criminal smugglers, and defector pilots operating from hidden bases. Strengths: small-unit initiative, stolen technology, public sympathy among Rim worlds weakened by "order." Weaknesses: no manufacturing base, dependence on secrecy, hence the Empire's informant economy and base-hunting operations.

#### Alliance Cell Structure (intelligence assessment) **[W]**

| Element | Function |
|---|---|
| High Command (council) | Fleet decisions; location changes with every leak |
| Fighter squadrons | Red/Gold/Blue group designations; veteran cadre |
| Sector cells | Partisan networks on occupied worlds |
| Mon Cal conversion yards | Capital-ship manufacturing from civilian liners — the rebellion's shipyards |
| Bothan spynet **[E]** | Intelligence network; every major Imperial secret they've delivered has cost them dearly |

**Assessment caveat:** official figures understate. Every ISD captain who has dismissed "farmers with stolen fighters" as a threat has contributed to the Rebellion's veteran pool.

### 6.2 The Force

An energy field formerly mediated by the Jedi Order; now an Inquisitorius hunting ground. Force-sensitive individuals are strategic assets or threats depending on loyalty screening. The Emperor's interest in Force matters is above Navy pay grade.

### 6.3 Crime Syndicates

Hutt-space cartels: tolerated so long as tribute flows and rebellion doesn't route through their lanes.

---

## PART SEVEN: COMMAND ORGANIZATION

```
THE EMPEROR
        |
IMPERIAL HIGH COMMAND —— DARTH VADER (fleet death squad authority)
        |
SECTOR GROUPS   (Moff-governed; ~24 ISDs + support per sector **[E]**)
        |
SYSTEMS FORCES · CONVOY PATROLS · GARRISONS
```

- **Moffs** wield sector-level civil and military power, the Empire's speed and its succession risk.
- **Vader's flotilla** operates outside sector chains; officers who fail him do not repeat.
- **Stormtrooper Corps** answers through Army channels; loyalty indoctrination begins at conscription age.

---

## PART EIGHT: IMPERIAL BESTIARY

### 8.0 The Force

**What:** The binding field of the galaxy; sensibles number one in millions.
**Cost of contact:** Discipline or death. The Emperor prefers recruitment.

### 8.2 Uncharted Regions

**What:** The galactic west, unmapped, uncolonized, expensive to survey.
**Cost of contact:** Unknown. The Navy has lost probe droids there and reports none.

### 8.3 Hutt Space

**What:** Semi-autonomous cartel territory the Empire taxes rather than governs.
**Cost of contact:** Diplomacy conducted in bribes and hostages.

### 8.4 Kessel Run / Smuggling Corridors

**What:** Illegal shipping routes threading mines and anomalies; measured in parsecs of shortcut bravado.
**Cost of contact:** Contraband, credits, and occasionally princesses.

---

<!-- ====== VOLUME: WARHAMMER 40,000 ====== -->

# VOLUME: WARHAMMER 40,000

*M41.999 · the Great Rift era*

---

# WARHAMMER 40,000: IMPERIAL NAVY MANUAL

**DEPARTMENTO MUNITORUM: FLEET OPERATIONS SANCTIONED TEXT**

*Twelfth and final entry in the collected set; house-style variant: authored as an Imperial sanctioned text. In the 41st Millennium, canon is contradictory by design, this manual rules disputes with heavy [W] latitude and liturgical confidence.*

---

## ABOUT THIS SANCTIONED TEXT

This text instructs officers of the Imperial Navy in the conduct of void war across the God-Emperor's dominion. Knowledge is a weapon; ignorance is heresy; this volume is therefore loaded.

**Edition pin.** Current as of **M41.999, the era of the Great Rift, the Thirteenth Black Crusade broken at Cadia's grave** (see §1.5).

**Conventions:**

| Marker | Meaning |
|---|---|
| *(no marker)* | Sanctioned doctrine (as verified by the Departmento — within its means) |
| **[E]** | Estimated; sector archives vary |
| **[W]** | Latitude note — the Imperium is contradictory on purpose |

---

## PART ONE: THE THEATER

### 1.0 Introduction

There is only war. The Imperium of Man spans a million worlds held by faith, fleet, and the corpses of both. The Navy is the rope binding a million scattered candles against a night with teeth.

### 1.1 Strategic Situation

| Property | Value |
|---|---|
| Theater | The Milky Way — Segmentae Solar, Obscurus, Ultima, Tempestus, Pacificus |
| Threats | Chaos (the Archenemy), Ork WAAAGH!s, Tyranid hive fleets, Necron dynasties, T'au expansion, Aeldari machinations, heresy within |
| Posture | Total war everywhere, forever; victory defined as continued existence |

Defining facts:

1. **The Astronomican lights the way**, the Emperor's psychic beacon, ten thousand years burning, guiding Navigators through the Warp.
2. **The Warp is both road and predator.** All FTL rides its tides; every jump risks soul-consumption.
3. **The Imperium cannot lose quickly or win slowly.** Attrition is strategy; martyrdom is logistics.

### 1.2 Consolidated Timeline

| Era | Event |
|---|---|
| M25–M29 | Dark Age of Technology; humanity's machine golden age ends in catastrophe |
| M30 | The Emperor's Great Crusade reunifies the galaxy; the Horus Heresy betrays it at its zenith |
| M31–M40 | Ten millennia of siege-state: the Imperium ossifies into faith and attrition |
| M41.999 | Great Rift tears the galaxy; Cadia falls; the Thirteenth Black Crusade breaks. **Pin** |

### 1.3 Threat Powers (Summary)

Chaos corrupts, Orks swarm, Tyranids devour, Necrons wake, Aeldari scheme, five apocalypses queued, each certain it is first in line. Full dossiers: Part Six.

### 1.4 Founding Technologies

**In brief:** Three miracles hold the Imperium together: Navigators reading the Astronomican through the Warp's madness, Gellar fields keeping souls aboard ships that jump, and void shields/lance batteries making cathedrals seaworthy in hell. All are maintained by a priesthood that worships what it cannot reproduce.

### 1.5 Status Quo Snapshot (M41.999)

| Domain | Status |
|---|---|
| The Rift | Cicatrix Maledictum torn across the galaxy; Warp bleeds into realspace |
| Cadia | Destroyed ("Cadia stands before it fell") |
| Fleet posture | Battlefleet mobilization galaxy-wide; reinforcements arrive decades late or centuries early |
| Communication | Astropathic choir messages — psychic, allegorical, unreliable |

---

## PART TWO: BATTLEFLEET OPERATIONS

### 2.0 The Imperial Ship

**In brief:** Imperial capital vessels are cathedral-fortresses: kilometers of gothic armor, plasma drives the size of towns, crews in the tens of thousands, and a lifespan measured in millennia of service and retrofit.

| Property | Typical values |
|---|---|
| Escort (Sword-class frigate) | ~1.6 km; crew ~25,000? **[E]** — sources conflict gloriously |
| Cruiser (Lunar/Mars class) | ~5 km; lance batteries + macro-cannon broadsides |
| Battleship (Retribution/Emperor class) | ~8 km+; Nova cannons; attack bays; cathedral spires functional as gun shields |

```
   IMPERIAL CRUISER — PROFILE (schematic)

   [PROWL: armored ram · torpedo tubes]
      \\   gothic spires (sensor shrines)
       \\______ lance turrets ____
       |   macro-battery decks    |
       |   cathedrals · holds     |
       |__ plasma drive blocks ___/
          [STERN: engine cathedrals]
```

### 2.2 Void Combat Doctrine

- **Broadside geometry:** ships fight broadside-on, presenting maximum guns and minimum profile, Age of Sail with plasma.
- **Lances vs. batteries:** lances burn through void shields point-first; macro shells saturate.
- **Void shields:** layered warp-membranes that collapse under saturation and recharge after, timing windows decide duels.
- **Boarding actions:** holy and common; armsmen hold corridors while tech-adeptes defend the enginarium.

### 2.3 Cruiser Departments (command orientation)

| Department | Function | Personnel |
|---|---|---|
| The Bridge / command spire | Lord-Captain's authority, Navigator's sanctum adjoining | Officers, servitor banks |
| Gunnery decks | Macro-battery crews by the thousand; shell lifts from magazines | Pressed crews, gun-marshals |
| Lance citadels | Beam capacitor banks and focusing shrines | Tech-adeptes |
| Enginarium | Plasma drives, reactor shrines | Mechanicus priesthood only **[W]** |
| Life decks | Crew habitation — hereditary ship families evolve within a generation | Population of a town, born aboard |
| Launch bays | Fury interceptors, Starhawk bombers | Wing commanders, flight chaplains |

**Crew culture:** ships are dynasties. Families live and die in the same corridors for centuries; mutiny is heresy, and heresy is contagious, hence the Commissariat.
---

## PART THREE: THE WARP AND NAVIGATION

**In brief:** All FTL transits the Warp, a psychic dimension where emotion is weather and predators are native. Transit requires:

1. **Navigators**, mutated human bloodlines whose third eye perceives the Astronomican.
2. **Gellar fields**, reality membranes keeping the ship (and souls) intact inside the sea of souls.
3. **Astropathic choirs**, psychic messaging; allegorical, delayed, occasionally screaming.

**Hazards:** warp storms (regions severed for centuries), time dilation (arriving before departure happens), and daemonic incursion when Gellars fail. Every jump is a prayer with mathematics attached.

---

## PART FOUR: FLEET ELEMENTS

| Element | Role |
|---|---|
| Battlefleet (segmentum) | Cruisers + battleships; sector defense |
| Escort squadrons | Frigates/destroyers: convoy, screening, martyrdom |
| Attack craft wings | Fury interceptors, Starhawk bombers from flight decks |
| Transport convoys | The Guard's lifeline; ten billion guardsmen moved per decade **[E]** |
| Space Marine strike cruisers/battle barges | Adeptus Astartes independent operations; one company retakes worlds |

### 4.5 Enemy Fleet Profiles

| Enemy | Doctrine | Counter |
|---|---|---|
| Chaos warbands | Daemon-engines, corrupted hulls, sorcery | Faith, torpedoes, exterminatus after |
| Ork roks/WAAAGH! | Ramming as philosophy; scrap-fleet tonnage | Distance, gunnery, post-battle sweeps |
| Tyranid hive fleets | Bio-ships in millions; adaptation | Exterminatus before spores land |
| Necron tomb fleets | Inertialess tech, gauss flensing | Withdraw and notify Mars |

---

## PART FIVE: WEAPONS AND SANCTIONED ARMAMENT

- **Macro-cannons:** massed shell broadsides; reload crews by the thousand.
- **Lance batteries:** sustained beam cuts through shields and armor alike.
- **Nova cannon:** spinal mass driver firing at extreme range, misses are still area events.
- **Torpedoes:** crewed or uncrewed; some carry plasma, some carry worse.
- **Exterminatus:** cyclonic torpedoes/virus bombardment rendering worlds lifeless, authorization: Inquisition/Lord High Admiral tier. The Navy's final argument.

### 5.6 Ground Forces (transport responsibility)

The Astra Militarum (Guard): billions of regiments; the Navy delivers them into the fire on schedule. Astartes: eight-foot transhuman monks in ceramite, transport them respectfully.

---

## PART SIX: THREAT DOSSIERS

### 6.0 Chaos and the Archenemy

**In brief:** The Warp's gods (Khorne, Tzeentch, Nurgle, Slaanesh) cultivate mortal corruption: traitor legions (the Horus Heresy's heirs, ten millennia traitor), daemon engines, cults within every hive world. The Black Crusades test the Cadian Gate every generation.

**Engagement rules:** Purity of faith is armor. Never board a derelict without a priest. Never read what the enemy writes.

#### The Four Great Enemies of the Soul

| Power | Domain | Temptation |
|---|---|---|
| Khorne | Blood, war, rage | Violence offered as strength |
| Tzeentch | Change, scheming, hope | Knowledge and "just one more plan" |
| Nurgle | Decay, despair, endurance through rot | Relief from pain and fear of death |
| Slaanesh | Excess, sensation, perfection | Everything beautiful taken one step too far |

Each god fields traitor legions, daemon engines, and cults. The Traitor Legions of the Horus Heresy remain their champions: ten millennia traitor, still fighting the battle they lost at Terra's gates.

### 6.2 The Tyranid Hive Fleets

Extragalactic devourers: bio-fleets consuming biospheres, adapting to every wound. Each fleet destroyed reveals three more inbound beyond the galactic rim. Genestealer cults precede them by generations, infiltration as appetite.

### 6.3 Orks

Fungal war-race: spore-reproducing, technology that works because they believe it does, WAAAGH!s that turn aggression into physics. Endless, cheerful, unstoppable except by greater enthusiasm plus superior firepower.

### 6.4 Necrons & Aeldari

Elder races waking (Necron dynasties) and dying (Aeldari craftworlds) around the Imperium's edges; both possess technology the Mechanicus covets and cannot replicate. Treat contact as archaeology with lasers.
---

## PART SEVEN: COMMAND ORGANIZATION

```
THE GOD-EMPEROR (Throne, Terra — ten millennia on the Golden Throne)
        |
HIGH LORDS OF TERRA      — Imperium governance (12 seats)
        |
LORD HIGH ADMIRAL        — Imperial Navy
        |
BATTLEFLEET SEGMENTUM    → BATTLEFLEET SOLAR/OBSCURUS/ULTIMA...
        |                     (Sector · sub-sector · convoy commands)
ADEPTUS ASTARTES         — independent; answers to the Throne alone
INQUISITION              — authority above all; oversight of everyone
```

- **The Inquisition outranks everything** it chooses to. Ordo Hereticus (witches), Xenos (aliens), Malleus (daemons).
- **The Adeptus Mechanicus** owns all technology: Fleet tech-adeptes are loaned clergy, not crew. Mars and Terra are partners in a theocratic duopoly.
- **Faith is force multiplication:** liturgy precedes launch; morale is doctrine.

---

## PART EIGHT: A GRIM BESTIARY

### 8.0 The Warp

**What:** The sea of souls underlying reality, realm of daemons, mirror of emotion, highway of empire.
**Where:** Beneath everything.
**Cost of contact:** Your soul, eventually. Gellar fields are the only umbrella.

### 8.2 The Astronomican

**What:** The Emperor's psychic lighthouse, powered by a thousand psykers' daily sacrifice.
**Where:** Terra, visible to Navigators across the galaxy.
**Cost of contact:** It burns a thousand souls a day so a million worlds can find their way home. The arithmetic of the Imperium in one sentence.

### 8.3 Exterminatus

**What:** World-killing sanction: cyclonic torpedoes or virus bombardment erasing biospheres to deny them to the enemy.
**Where:** Anywhere heresy wins.
**Cost of contact:** A world. The Inquisition keeps count; the count has no bottom.

### 8.4 Genestealer Cults

**What:** Tyranid vanguard infiltrations, four-armed prophets building insurrections generations before the fleet arrives.
**Where:** Undercity altars on every productive world.
**Cost of contact:** The uprising is the appetizer.

### 8.5 The Golden Throne

**What:** The Emperor's life-support and psychic anchor: a device consuming a thousand psykers daily to keep the master of mankind half-alive, holding shut the door his greatest son broke.
**Where:** The Imperial Palace, Terra, inner sanctum.
**Cost of contact:** Pilgrims die by the thousand for a glimpse of the outer gate. The Throne is failing; the Mechanicus patches it; nobody says what happens when the patching stops. **[W]**

### 8.6 The Black Library **[W]**

**What:** The Aeldari harlequins' hidden archive of everything Chaos is and was, the only complete map of the enemy, guarded so absolutely that its location is itself a myth.
**Where:** The Webway.
**Cost of contact:** The Imperium denies it exists. The Inquisition's oldest files disagree with the Imperium.

---

## APPENDICES

### Appendix A: Glossary

| Term | Definition |
|---|---|
| Astropath | Psychic messenger; choir member |
| Astronomican | Emperor's psychic beacon (§8.2) |
| Commissar | Morality officer; executes discipline personally |
| Exterminatus | World-destruction sanction (§8.3) |
| Gellar field | Warp-reality membrane protecting ships |
| Navigator | Mutant bloodline steering warp transit |
| Void shields | Layered warp-membrane defenses |

### Appendix B: Travel Times

| Route | Duration |
|---|---|
| Segmentum crossing | Months–years; variance extreme **[E]** |
| Neighboring systems | Weeks–months |
| Messages (astropathic) | Variable; allegory included at no charge |

### Appendix C: Discrepancy Log / Canonical Contradiction Ruling

The setting's contradictions are canon: every source is an in-universe document of variable reliability. This manual therefore rules:

| Topic | Ruling |
|---|---|
| Ship dimensions (conflicting sources) | All may be true — different ships, different forges, lying scribes **[W]** |
| Imperial Guard numbers | "Billions" always; precision is heresy |
| Warp travel time | "Arrives when the Emperor wills" plus navigation check |
| Horus Heresy details | Ten thousand years of retelling; treat variants as sermons |

**Writer's latitude:** maximal. Grimdark thrives on contradiction rendered with total confidence.
